<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Gersten's conjecture: every finitely generated one-relator group containing no subgroup isomorphic to a Baumslag–Solitar group $`\mathop{\mathrm{BS}}\nolimits (m,n)`$, for nonzero integers <i>m</i>, <i>n</i>, is word-hyperbolic.

- **claim-one-relator-hyperbolicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a finite set and let $r\in F(X)$. If the one-relator group $G=\langle X\mid r\rangle$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $\operatorname{BS}(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$, then $G$ is word-hyperbolic.
  - Explanation: A finite one-relator presentation defines a word-hyperbolic group under the stated exclusion of Baumslag–Solitar subgroups. The statement permits trivial relators and proper powers.
- **claim-magnus-subgraph-quasiconvexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-magnus)): Let $\Gamma$ be a finite connected graph, and let $\lambda:S^1\to\Gamma$ be a nontrivial immersed circuit representing a non-power in $\pi_1\Gamma$. Attach a $2$-cell along $\lambda$, obtaining $Y$. If $\pi_1Y$ is Baumslag–Solitar-free, then, for every connected subgraph $S\subseteq\Gamma$ with $\lambda(S^1)\nsubseteq S$, the map $$\pi_1S\longrightarrow\pi_1Y$$ is injective and its image is quasiconvex.
  - Explanation: Connected subgraphs omitting part of the attaching circuit inject with quasiconvex image when the graph presentation satisfies the non-power and subgroup-exclusion hypotheses.
- **claim-quasiconvex-intersections** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-quasiconvex-intersection)): Let $K,L$ be quasiconvex subgroups of a finitely generated hyperbolic group $H$. Then $K\cap L$ is quasiconvex and $$\Lambda(K\cap L)=\Lambda K\cap\Lambda L.$$ The same assertions hold for any finite family of quasiconvex subgroups.
  - Explanation: Finite intersections retain quasiconvexity, and their boundary limit sets equal the intersections of the constituent limit sets.
- **claim-finite-intersection-double-cosets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-double-coset-finiteness)): Let $K,L$ be quasiconvex subgroups of a finitely generated hyperbolic group $H$. There are only finitely many double cosets $KgL$ such that $K\cap gLg^{-1}$ contains an infinite-order element. If $H$ is torsion-free, there are therefore only finitely many such double cosets with nontrivial intersection.
  - Explanation: Only finitely many double cosets support an intersection containing an infinite-order element. Torsion-freeness permits replacement of that condition by nontriviality.
- **claim-cyclic-commensurator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-cyclic-commensurator)): If $C$ is an infinite cyclic subgroup of a hyperbolic group $H$, then $C$ has finite index in $\operatorname{Comm}_H(C)$. If $H$ is torsion-free, this commensurator is infinite cyclic.
  - Explanation: An infinite cyclic subgroup has finite index in its commensurator. The commensurator is itself infinite cyclic in the torsion-free case.
- **claim-cyclic-tree-stabilizer-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-gbs-subgroup)): Let $Q$ be a finitely generated noncyclic group acting on a simplicial tree without inversions. If every vertex and edge stabilizer is infinite cyclic, then $Q$ contains a subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for some nonzero integers $m,n$.
  - Explanation: A finitely generated noncyclic group with the specified cyclic tree stabilizers contains an embedded Baumslag–Solitar subgroup.
- **claim-elliptic-commensuration-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-elliptic-commensurator)): Suppose that $G$ acts on a simplicial tree $\mathcal T$ without inversions, with torsion-free hyperbolic vertex stabilizers. If an infinite-order elliptic element $a\in G$ has its cyclic subgroup commensurated by a tree-hyperbolic element $g\in G$, then $G$ contains a subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for some nonzero integers $m,n$.
  - Explanation: Commensuration of an infinite elliptic cyclic subgroup by a tree-hyperbolic element yields an embedded Baumslag–Solitar subgroup under the vertex-stabilizer hypotheses.
- **claim-strong-inertia-off-diagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-cyclic-off-diagonal)): Let $P$ be a finitely generated free group and $E\leq P$ a strongly inert subgroup. If $g\in P\setminus E$, then $E\cap gEg^{-1}$ is trivial or cyclic.
  - Explanation: Strong inertia forces every off-diagonal self-conjugate intersection to be trivial or cyclic.
- **claim-restricted-hnn-combination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-combination)): Let $H$ be a torsion-free word-hyperbolic group, let $A,B\leq H$ be finitely generated free quasiconvex subgroups, and let $\phi\colon A\to B$ be an isomorphism. Set $$G=\langle H,t\mid tat^{-1}=\phi(a)\ (a\in A)\rangle, \qquad P=A,\qquad I=A\cap B,\qquad J=t^{-1}It\leq P.$$ Assume the following two conditions.* 1. *For $M,N\in\{A,B\}$ and $g\in H$, the intersection $M\cap gNg^{-1}$ is at most cyclic unless $g\in MN$.* 2. *The groups $I,J$ are finitely generated, and each $E\in\{I,J\}$ satisfies $$\sum_{SpE\in S\backslash P/E} \bar r(E\cap p^{-1}Sp)\leq\bar r(S) \qquad\text{for every finitely generated }S\leq P. \label{ann:inertia-assumption}$$* *If $G$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$, then $G$ is word-hyperbolic.
  - Explanation: The HNN extension is hyperbolic when the vertex and edge hypotheses, the double-coset intersection restriction, the separate inertia inequalities, and Baumslag–Solitar exclusion all hold.
- **claim-aligned-frames** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-frames)): A reduced path in $\mathcal T$ with noncyclic pointwise stabilizer has constant traversal sign. The positive successors of $e$ that can occur in such a path have the form $pte$, with $pI\in P/I$, and the positive predecessors have the form $pt^{-1}e$, with $pJ\in P/J$. Their common stabilizers with $e$ are, respectively, $$pIp^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad pJp^{-1}.$$ For a positive successor the coordinate transfer is $c\mapsto t^{-1}p^{-1}cpt$; for a positive predecessor it is $c\mapsto tp^{-1}cpt^{-1}$, on the indicated domains.
  - Explanation: Noncyclic pointwise stabilizers force constant traversal sign. The allowable neighboring edges and their coordinate transfers are expressed by the overlap cosets.
- **claim-fixed-window-rank-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-ann-window-bound)): Fix integers $\ell,r\geq0$. Consider the positively traversed reduced paths with edges indexed by $-\ell,\ldots,r$, with edge $0$ equal to $e$, and with noncyclic pointwise stabilizer. Then $$\sum_{[W]\in P\backslash\{\text{such paths}\}} \bar r\bigl(\operatorname{Stab}_G(W)\bigr) \leq\bar r(P). \label{ann:window-sum}$$ Every stabilizer in this sum is finitely generated and free. In particular, there are at most $\bar r(P)$ such $P$-orbits. Equivalently, the same bound holds for $G$-orbits if the distinguished edge is allowed to vary.
  - Explanation: For a fixed window shape, the total reduced rank of noncyclic stabilizers is bounded by the reduced rank of the edge group. This also bounds the orbit count.
- **claim-distinct-window-cyclic-overlap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-distinct-paths)): Two distinct aligned paths of the same fixed window shape with distinguished edge $e$ and the same traversal sign have at most cyclic common pointwise stabilizer.
  - Explanation: Distinct aligned paths with matching shape, distinguished edge, and traversal sign have at most cyclic common stabilizer.
- **claim-annular-flaring-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ann-external-flaring)): Let $H$ be word-hyperbolic, let $A,B\leq H$ be finitely generated, and let $\phi:A\to B$ be an isomorphism such that both attaching maps $A\to H$ are quasi-isometric embeddings. Set $G=\langle H,t\mid tat^{-1}=\phi(a),\ a\in A\rangle$. Then $G$ is word-hyperbolic if there exist $\lambda>1$ and an integer $m\geq1$ such that, for every $\rho>0$, there is $h(\rho)$ for which every essential algebraic annulus as defined above, with $2m+1$ rows, width at most $\rho$, and girth at least $h(\rho)$, is $\lambda$-hyperbolic.
  - Explanation: Uniform expansion and a fixed row count establish hyperbolicity, while the girth threshold is allowed to depend on the width bound.
- **claim-fixed-length-counterannuli** (construction, [source](#stmt-ann-counterannuli)): If $G$ is not hyperbolic, then for every $m\geq1$ there is a sequence of essential annuli with $2m+1$ rows whose signs and bridges are fixed throughout the sequence, such that $$\|c_0\|_P\longrightarrow\infty, \qquad \|c_{-m}\|_P,\|c_m\|_P<2\|c_0\|_P.$$ For each fixed $s\in[-m,m]$, the ratios $\|c_s\|_P/\|c_0\|_P$ are bounded. The bounds and the fixed bridge tuple may depend on $m$.
  - Explanation: Nonhyperbolicity supplies counterannuli with fixed signs and bridges at each fixed row count. Neither the bridges nor the intermediate ratio bounds are asserted to be uniform across row counts.
- **claim-current-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ann-current-compactness)): The norm is continuous on $\operatorname{Curr}(F)$, and each closed norm-bounded subset is compact. In particular, every bounded sequence of currents has a convergent subsequence.
  - Explanation: The current norm is continuous, and closed norm-bounded sets are compact in the cylinder topology.
- **claim-current-coset-induction** (construction, [source](#stmt-ann-current-induction)): Let $\theta\colon Q\to F$ be an injection of nontrivial finite-rank free groups. It defines a continuous linear map $$\theta_*\colon\operatorname{Curr}(Q)\longrightarrow \operatorname{Curr}(F)$$ which sends $\eta_w$ to $\eta_{\theta(w)}$ and sends every nonzero current to a nonzero current. For the fixed bases there are constants $0<c_\theta\leq C_\theta<\infty$ such that $$c_\theta\\|\nu\\|_Q\leq\\|\theta_*\nu\\|_F \leq C_\theta\\|\nu\\|_Q \qquad(\nu\in\operatorname{Curr}(Q)). \label{ann:induction-norm}$$ Postcomposing $\theta$ with an inner automorphism of $F$ does not change $\theta_*$. Induction is functorial: if $\psi\colon F\to F'$ is another injection of nontrivial finite-rank free groups, then $$(\psi\circ\theta)_*=\psi_*\circ\theta_*.$$
  - Explanation: An injection induces a continuous linear current map by summing boundary pushes over target cosets. The map preserves nonzero currents, obeys norm comparisons, ignores target inner conjugation, and composes functorially.
- **claim-linear-word-capping** (construction, [source](#stmt-ann-capping)): Fix an injection $\theta\colon Q\to F$ of nontrivial finite-rank free groups with fixed bases. For each nonempty reduced word $W$ in $Q$, there is a suffix $b$ of length at most one such that $Wb$ is cyclically reduced. For every fixed nonempty reduced word $u$ in $F$ there is a constant $D_{\theta,u}$, independent of $W$, such that $$\left\|\eta_{\theta(Wb)} \bigl(\operatorname{Cyl}_F([1,u])\bigr) -N_u^{\mathrm{sym}}\bigl(\operatorname{red}(\theta(W))\bigr) \right\|\leq D_{\theta,u}. \label{ann:capping-frequency}$$ There is also a constant $D_\theta$ independent of $W$ such that $$\bigl\|\\|\eta_{\theta(Wb)}\\|_F-\|\theta(W)\|_F\bigr\| \leq D_\theta. \label{ann:capping-length}$$ Here $\operatorname{red}$ denotes the unique reduced representative. The same cap $b$ may be used simultaneously for any finite collection of fixed injections out of $Q$.
  - Explanation: A common short cap makes a reduced source word cyclically reduced. For each fixed injection and test word, the resulting counting-current statistics differ from linear word statistics by bounded end errors.
- **claim-finite-current-window** (construction, [source](#stmt-ann-finite-current-window)): Suppose $G$ is not hyperbolic, and fix $m\geq1$. There are currents $\mu_s\in\operatorname{Curr}(P)$ for $-m\leq s\leq m$ with $$\\|\mu_0\\|=1,\qquad \\|\mu_{-m}\\|,\\|\mu_m\\|\leq2, \label{ann:finite-end-bounds}$$ and a fixed essential sign and bridge tuple such that each consecutive pair is induced by a current on its intersection subgroup. More precisely, for $$K_s=f_{\epsilon_s}(P)\cap a_s l_{\epsilon_{s+1}}(P)a_s^{-1}$$ there is $\nu_s\in\operatorname{Curr}(K_s)$ satisfying $$(\theta_{s,0})_*\nu_s=\mu_s, \qquad(\theta_{s,1})_*\nu_s=\mu_{s+1}, \label{ann:finite-compatibility}$$ where $$\theta_{s,0}=f_{\epsilon_s}^{-1},\qquad \theta_{s,1}=l_{\epsilon_{s+1}}^{-1} \circ(c\mapsto a_s^{-1}ca_s).$$ All the groups $K_s$ are nontrivial finitely generated free groups.
  - Explanation: At each fixed row count, normalized capped counterannuli converge to compatible row and transition currents with nonzero central norm and bounded end norms.
- **claim-finite-transition-types** (classification, [source](#stmt-ann-finite-types)): Every consecutive pair of currents from Lemma <a href="#ann:finite-current-window" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:finite-current-window">19</a> can be expressed as $$\mu_s=(\theta_{\tau_s,0})_*\nu_s, \qquad \mu_{s+1}=(\theta_{\tau_s,1})_*\nu_s$$ for a current on one of the fixed groups $K_{\tau_s}$, with type signs $(\epsilon_s,\epsilon_{s+1})$. There are constants $C\geq2$ and $D\geq1$, independent of $m$, such that $$C^{-1}\\|\mu_s\\|\leq\\|\mu_{s+1}\\|\leq C\\|\mu_s\\|, \qquad \\|\nu_s\\|\leq D\\|\mu_s\\|. \label{ann:uniform-transition-bounds}$$ If $K_\tau$ is noncyclic, the signs are equal and $d=1$. For the positive aligned type the two maps are $i,j$, and for the negative aligned type they are $j,i$.
  - Explanation: After taking fixed-window limits, bridge conjugacies reduce transitions to a finite type list with norm comparisons independent of window length.
- **claim-bounded-bilateral-current-trajectory** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ann-trajectory)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#ann:combination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:combination">10</a>, if $G$ is not hyperbolic, there exist currents $\mu_s\in\operatorname{Curr}(P)$ for $s\in\mathbb Z$, signs $\epsilon_s\in\{+,-\}$, and transition types $\tau_s$ with signs $(\epsilon_s,\epsilon_{s+1})$, such that* 1. *$\\|\mu_0\\|=1$, every $\mu_s$ is nonzero, and $\sup_{s\in\mathbb Z}\\|\mu_s\\|<\infty$;* 2. *for every $s$ there is a current $\nu_s\in\operatorname{Curr}(K_{\tau_s})$ with $$\mu_s=(\theta_{\tau_s,0})_*\nu_s, \qquad \mu_{s+1}=(\theta_{\tau_s,1})_*\nu_s;$$* 3. *all types are essential: any reversing type uses a double coset outside the common side subgroup.* *The constants in <a href="#ann:uniform-transition-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ann:uniform-transition-bounds">[ann:uniform-transition-bounds]</a> apply to this trajectory. In particular, $\\|\mu_s\\|\geq C^{-\|s\|}$ for all $s$.
  - Explanation: Fixed-window trajectories yield a bilateral nonzero bounded trajectory. Recentering at maximal norm prevents divergent finite-window maxima from destroying the bilateral limit.
- **claim-atoms-are-periodic-axes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-atomic-axis)): Every atom of a free-group current is a periodic axis. If the mass of one oriented axis is $b>0$ and its root is $r$, then its translate orbit, together with the reversed orientations, contributes $b\|r\|_{\mathrm{cyc}}$ to the current norm.
  - Explanation: Local finiteness forces an atomic line to have a nontrivial translation stabilizer. Its translate orbit contributes a norm determined by its mass and primitive cyclic length.
- **claim-finite-exceptional-root-dictionary** (classification, [source](#stmt-stat-finite-roots)): There is a finite set $\mathcal D$ of root classes with the following properties.* 1. *The root of either image of a nontrivial element of a cyclic transition group belongs to $\mathcal D$.* 2. *If a periodic axis is carried by $p\Lambda E$ and $q\Lambda E$ for distinct cosets $pE\ne qE$, where $E=I$ or $E=J$, its root belongs to $\mathcal D$.* 3. *If an axis root in a noncyclic transition group maps to a proper power under either transition injection, the root of that image belongs to $\mathcal D$.* *Here “carried” means that both endpoints lie in the indicated translate of the limit set.
  - Explanation: A finite root dictionary captures cyclic transitions, ambiguous overlap cosets, and proper-power images of transition roots.
- **claim-repeated-roots-force-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-repetition)): Let $(h_s e)$ be a reduced edge path in $\mathcal T$, indexed by consecutive integers, and let $r_s\in P$ be nontrivial. Suppose the cyclic subgroups $$h_s\langle r_s\rangle h_s^{-1}$$ at consecutive positions are commensurable. If $i<j$ have the same traversal sign and $\langle r_i\rangle,\langle r_j\rangle$ are conjugate in $P$, then $G$ contains a Baumslag–Solitar subgroup.
  - Explanation: Repeated conjugate cyclic subgroups with matching traversal signs along a reduced path produce a tree-hyperbolic commensurator and hence a Baumslag–Solitar subgroup.
- **claim-bounded-trajectories-atomless** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-no-atoms)): Let $(\mu_s)_{s\in\mathbb Z}$ be a bounded sequence of nonzero $P$-currents with transition currents and compatible essential types as in Proposition <a href="#ann:trajectory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:trajectory">21</a>. If $G$ is Baumslag–Solitar free, every $\mu_s$ is atomless.
  - Explanation: An atom propagates through the trajectory. Exceptional roots or a persistent positive atomic mass force a repeated root class, contradicting Baumslag–Solitar exclusion.
- **claim-nonzero-stationary-current** (construction, [source](#stmt-stat-stationary)): Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href="#stat:no-atoms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stat:no-atoms">25</a>, there exist a nonzero current $\nu$ on $I$ and a nonzero atomless current $\mu$ on $P$ such that $$\label{stat:stationary-equality} i_*\nu=j_*\nu=\mu.$$ In particular, if the restricted HNN extension is not hyperbolic, Proposition <a href="#ann:trajectory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:trajectory">21</a> supplies such a stationary current.
  - Explanation: Normalized averaging handles divergent total transition mass, while a positive bilateral sum handles summable mass. Atomlessness is established again after obtaining stationarity.
- **claim-irrational-endpoints-almost-everywhere** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-irrational)): For an atomless free-group current, almost every line has two irrational endpoints. An injection between finitely generated free groups preserves and reflects rationality of boundary points.
  - Explanation: Atomlessness excludes rational endpoints almost everywhere. Injective homomorphisms of finitely generated free groups preserve and reflect endpoint rationality.
- **claim-conull-invariant-transfers** (construction, [source](#stmt-stat-transfers)): There is a translation- and flip-invariant conull Borel set $\mathcal U\subset\Omega$ with the following properties.* 1. *Every line of $\mathcal U$ has two irrational endpoints and is carried by a unique $I$ coset and a unique $J$ coset.* 2. *On lines carried by $p\Lambda I$, translate by $p^{-1}$ and apply the boundary isomorphism from $I$ to $J$. This forward transfer, and the analogous backward transfer from $q\Lambda J$, preserve $\mu$ on their domains.* 3. *Every defined transfer, its inverse, and every output frame translation take lines of $\mathcal U$ to lines of $\mathcal U$. Consequently all successive forward and backward transfers exist on $\mathcal U$.* *Measure preservation in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> holds on the full Borel domain of lines with irrational endpoints, not only after restriction to $\mathcal U$.
  - Explanation: Removing a countable saturation of null exceptional sets gives a conull invariant Borel set with unique overlap carriers and indefinitely iterable measure-preserving transfers.
- **claim-actual-bilateral-window-trajectories** (construction, [source](#stmt-stat-windows)): Every line $L\in\mathcal U$ determines an actual aligned bilateral path of positively traversed edges $$\cdots,E_{-1},E_0=e,E_1,\cdots$$ in $\mathcal T$, with transferred lines in its successive edge frames. The following properties hold.* 1. *The path is independent of coset representatives and of compatible changes of frames and line coordinates.* 2. *For every finite window containing $E_0$, its stabilizer $K\le P$ is finitely generated and noncyclic, and the endpoints of $L$ lie in $\Lambda K$.* 3. *Two distinct actual aligned windows of the same shape about $e$ have at most cyclic common stabilizer. In particular, an irrational point can belong to the limit sets of at most one such window stabilizer.* 4. *If an aligned one-sided window joins $e$ to $he$, its stabilizer is $S=P\cap hPh^{-1}$. The boundary map of $$S\longrightarrow h^{-1}Sh, \qquad x\longmapsto h^{-1}xh,$$ implements its line transfer. On the irrational-endpoint domain it preserves $\mu$; it takes its domain in $\mathcal U$ into $\mathcal U$.* *In <span class="upright">(iii)</span>, a shape specifies the numbers of edges before and after the central edge, all traversed positively.
  - Explanation: Conull lines determine actual aligned bilateral paths. Their finite-window stabilizers carry the endpoints, are noncyclic, and provide the partial conjugations implementing transfer.
- **claim-local-window-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-stat-window-finiteness)): Fix a finite positive window shape about $e$ and a finite ball in the Cayley tree of $P$. There are only finitely many actual windows of that shape whose stabilizer is noncyclic and whose stabilizer limit set has convex hull meeting that ball.
  - Explanation: A fixed window shape has only finitely many actual noncyclic windows whose stabilizer hull meets a fixed finite ball.
- **claim-window-core-complexity** (bound, [source](#stmt-car-window-cores)): The following properties hold.* 1. *The sum of the reduced ranks of the components of $\Gamma_n$ is at most $r_0$. After suppressing degree-two interiors, $\Gamma_n$ has at most $3r_0$ unoriented edges and $2r_0$ vertices.* 2. *Every line in $\Omega$ has a label-preserving lift to $\Gamma_n$.* 3. *A ray with irrational endpoint has at most one starting state for a lift among all components of $\Gamma_n$.* *The same statements hold for any other fixed finite positive window shape containing its specified central edge, with the same bounds.
  - Explanation: Reduced-rank bounds control natural graph size after suppressing degree-two interiors. Support lines lift to every fixed core union, and an irrational ray has a unique starting state.
- **claim-no-proper-self-conjugate-containment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-car-conjugate-containment)): A nontrivial finitely generated subgroup of a finite-rank free group cannot be properly contained in a conjugate of itself. Consequently $\preceq$ is a partial order on $\mathcal C$.
  - Explanation: Finite core geometry rules out proper containment in a self-conjugate subgroup, making conjugacy containment a partial order on carrier classes.
- **claim-bounded-core-circuits** (bound, [source](#stmt-car-circuits)): Let $C$ be a finite connected graph with no degree-one vertices and with at least one edge. Every edge of $C$ lies on an immersed circuit of length at most $2\|E(C)\|$, where edges have length one and $\|E(C)\|$ counts unoriented edges.
  - Explanation: Every edge lies on a bounded immersed circuit, including a bridge joined to circuits on its two sides.
- **claim-finite-cofinal-carriers** (classification, [source](#stmt-car-finite-carriers)): The noncyclic component subgroup classes of $\Gamma^{\mathrm{sm}}$ belong to $\mathcal C$ and form a finite cofinal subset: every $[Q]\in\mathcal C$ is contained, up to $P$-conjugacy, in one of them. In particular $\mathcal C$ has only finitely many maximal elements, and they are exactly the maximal members of this finite family.
  - Explanation: Noncyclic small components provide a finite cofinal family for bilateral-path stabilizers. Maximal carrier classes are exactly the maximal members of that family.
- **claim-atoroidal-carrier-return** (construction, [source](#stmt-car-return)): The map $F$ is an order-preserving bijection with order-preserving inverse. It permutes the finite set of maximal classes of $\mathcal C$. For every representative $M$ of such a class, there are an integer $p\ge1$ and an element $v\in G$ of height $p$ such that $$v^{-1}Mv=M.$$ The automorphism $\alpha:M\to M$, $\alpha(m)=v^{-1}mv$, is atoroidal: no nontrivial conjugacy class in $M$ is periodic under $\alpha$.
  - Explanation: Forward transfer is an order automorphism on carrier classes. A return to a maximal class induces an onto atoroidal automorphism, with atoroidality forced by Baumslag–Solitar exclusion.
- **claim-return-expansion-on-currents** (bound, [source](#stmt-car-expansion)): Let $M,v,\alpha$ be as in Proposition <a href="#car:return" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="car:return">36</a>, and choose a free basis of $M$. There exist $b\ge1$, an automorphism $\beta=\alpha^b$, and $\lambda>4$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \lambda\|w\|_M &\le\max\{\|\beta(w)\|_M,\|\beta^{-1}(w)\|_M\}, &&w\in M, \label{car:based}\\ \lambda\ell_M(w) &\le\max\{\ell_M(\beta(w)),\ell_M(\beta^{-1}(w))\}, &&w\in M. \label{car:cyclic} \end{aligned}$$ For every $M$-current $\sigma$ one has $$\lambda\\|\sigma\\|_M \le\\|\beta_*\sigma\\|_M+ \\|(\beta^{-1})_*\sigma\\|_M. \label{car:current}$$* *The partial conjugations along the paths to $v^be$ and $v^{-b}e$ have domains $$S_+=P\cap v^bPv^{-b},\qquad S_-=P\cap v^{-b}Pv^b,$$ respectively. These are finitely generated full window stabilizers, and both contain $M$. Their boundary transfers are defined on all of $\partial^2\Lambda S_+$ and $\partial^2\Lambda S_-$. The transfers $\Theta_+,\Theta_-$ restrict on $\partial^2\Lambda M$ to $\beta,\beta^{-1}$, respectively, and preserve $\mu$ on their irrational-endpoint domains.
  - Explanation: An appropriate return power expands based and cyclic lengths. The current inequality uses a sum of forward and backward norms, obtained by approximating currents with counting-current combinations.
- **claim-maximal-carriers-zero-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-car-zero-mass)): For every maximal carrier $M$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#car:finite-carriers" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="car:finite-carriers">35</a>, $$\mu(\partial^2\Lambda M)=0.$$ The same holds for every $P$-translate of this line set.
  - Explanation: Measure preservation under both return directions contradicts expansion unless the restricted carrier current vanishes. This gives zero mass rather than exclusion from closed support.
- **claim-fixed-transfer-nearness-and-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-transport)): Let $a:Q\to F$ and $b:Q\to F'$ be injective homomorphisms between finitely generated free groups with fixed bases. For every $D\ge0$ there are constants $E,T\ge0$ with the following properties. Let $L$ be an oriented line of $\mathcal C_Q$, and let $L_a,L_b$ be its tightened image lines in $\mathcal C_F,\mathcal C_{F'}$. If $q\in Q$ satisfies $d(a(q),L_a)\le D$, then $$d(b(q),L_b)\le E.$$ If two such elements $q_1,q_2$ have projections to $L_a$ positively ordered and separated by more than $T$, then their projections to $L_b$ are in the same order. As their separation on $L_a$ tends to infinity, their separation on $L_b$ tends to infinity, uniformly over all $L,q_1,q_2$.
  - Explanation: Fixed free-group injections preserve bounded nearness to tightened lines and eventually preserve the order of sufficiently separated projections.
- **claim-expansion-with-uniform-trimming** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-trimming)): Fix a maximal carrier $M$ and the maps above. There are constants $Q\ge0$ and $L_*>0$ with the following property. Let $L$ be an oriented $M$-Cayley-tree line, let $x,y$ be vertices on $L$ with $d_M(x,y)=l\ge L_*$, and let $\mathcal A\subset\mathcal U$ be a Borel set whose lines lie within $Q$ of both $x,y$ in $\mathcal C_P$. Suppose a sign $\epsilon\in\{+1,-1\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\beta^\epsilon x,\beta^\epsilon y)\ge\lambda l.$$ If every line of $\mathcal A$ belongs to $\partial^2\Lambda S_\epsilon$, then its image $\mathcal A'=\Theta_\epsilon(\mathcal A)$ has the same measure, lies in $\mathcal U$, and admits vertices $x',y'$ on the tightened line $\beta^\epsilon L$ such that $$d_P(x',\ell),d_P(y',\ell)\le Q\quad(\ell\in\mathcal A'), \qquad d_M(x',y')\ge2l.$$ The same sign satisfies $$d_M(\beta^\epsilon x',\beta^\epsilon y') \ge\lambda d_M(x',y').$$ The constants are independent of $L,x,y,\mathcal A$ and of the number of times this assertion is applied.
  - Explanation: Fixed trimming retains uniform nearness to every transported line, preserves measure, and makes the expanding direction persist through repeated returns.
- **claim-finite-return-failure-periodic-overlap** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-exp-finite-failure)): There are constants $L_0\ge L_*$, $c>0$, $C_2\ge0$, and an integer $D\ge1$, depending only on the fixed splitting, carrier, and return maps, with the following property. Let $\eta\in\Omega\cap\partial^2\Lambda M$, let $L$ be its corresponding oriented $M$-Cayley-tree line, and choose vertices $x_0<y_0$ on $L$ with $l_0=d_M(x_0,y_0)\ge L_0$. Suppose $\epsilon\in\{+1,-1\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\beta^\epsilon x_0,\beta^\epsilon y_0)\ge\lambda l_0.$$ Then there are $n\ge0$, a line $\ell\in\mathcal U$, and an oriented periodic $P$-line $\zeta$ of primitive period at most $D$ such that $\ell$, $\zeta$, and the tightened $P$-image $\eta_n$ of $\beta^{\epsilon n}L$ share a segment of length at least $$c\,2^n l_0-C_2. \label{exp:failure-overlap}$$ The endpoints of $\zeta$ lie in the limit sets of the stabilizers of two distinct actual windows of the shape defining $\Theta_\epsilon$. One is the prescribed window, with stabilizer $S_\epsilon$.
  - Explanation: Positive mass prevents indefinite transport near a zero-mass carrier. A competing window at finite failure yields a long periodic overlap of uniformly bounded primitive period.
- **claim-carrier-support-forces-periodic-support** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-exp-carrier-periodic)): If $\Omega$ contains a line carried, up to translation, by a maximal noncyclic carrier $M$, then $\Omega$ contains a periodic line.
  - Explanation: A carrier line in the support produces bounded-period overlap axes. Recentering and closedness then place a periodic line in the support.
- **claim-bounded-whole-period-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-period-pullback)): Let $\psi$ be an automorphism of a finitely generated free group $M$ with a fixed basis. There is an integer $C_4\ge0$, depending only on $\psi$, with the following property. Suppose two lines $L_1,L_2$ of $\mathcal C_M$ share a segment $[u,z^k u]$, where $u$ is a vertex, $z$ translates $L_2$, and $k>C_4$. Their tightened $\psi$-images share a segment consisting of $k-C_4$ whole $\psi(z)$-periods. If $z$ is primitive as a translation in $M$, then so is $\psi(z)$.
  - Explanation: An automorphism loses only a bounded number of whole translation periods from a shared segment and preserves primitive translations.
- **claim-periodic-label-agreement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-exp-periodic-labels)): Two bi-infinite label sequences with respective positive integer periods $a,b$ that agree on $a+b$ consecutive positions agree everywhere. Consequently two oriented periodic lines in a labeled Cayley tree with such a common segment are the same actual line.
  - Explanation: Agreement over the stated sum of periods forces periodic label sequences to coincide globally and identifies the actual oriented Cayley-tree lines.
- **claim-bounded-period-axis-carrier-membership** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-carrier-period)): Let $M\le P$ be a finitely generated nontrivial subgroup, with fixed bases, and fix $D\ge1$. There are constants $L_D,B_D$ such that if a periodic $P$-line $\zeta$ of primitive period at most $D$ shares a segment of length at least $L_D$ with an $M$-line, then $\zeta$ is itself an $M$-line. Its primitive translation in $M$ has cyclic $M$-length at most $B_D$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently long overlap between a bounded-period axis and a carrier line places the entire axis in the carrier boundary and bounds its primitive carrier period.
- **claim-no-periodic-support-lines** (theorem, [source](#stmt-exp-no-periodic)): The support $\Omega$ contains no periodic line.
  - Explanation: A putative periodic support trajectory eventually has unique carrier returns. Pulling a failure axis back while counting whole periods identifies it with that trajectory and contradicts the competing window.
- **claim-entire-support-trajectories** (theorem, [source](#stmt-exp-support)): The following assertions hold for the closed support $\Omega$.* 1. *No line of $\Omega$ is carried by the small subgraph. There is an integer $B_0$ such that every segment of a lift of an $\Omega$-line to any of the selected subsequence cores $\Gamma_n$, if contained entirely in the small subgraph, has length at most $B_0$.* 2. *Every endpoint of every line of $\Omega$ is irrational.* 3. *Every line of $\Omega$ lifts to each centered core union $\Gamma_n$ and has a unique compatible bilateral trajectory. On each fixed window, the partial boundary transfer takes every support line in its domain to a line of $\Omega$.
  - Explanation: Excluding periodic support lines excludes small-graph carrier lines, bounds small-only segments, makes all support endpoints irrational, and extends compatible transfers to the entire support.
- **claim-finite-nonempty-special-rays** (classification, [source](#stmt-ray-finite)): The set of left-special occurring words is finite and nonempty.
  - Explanation: Bounded natural graph complexity and bounded small-only segments leave finitely many rays with distinct predecessors. Their absence would force periodic support.
- **claim-return-positive-lag** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ray-lag)): After replacing $\Theta$ by its inverse if necessary, the choices above may be made so that $$f(n)-n\longrightarrow+\infty.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded lag subsequence would create a nontrivial element commuting with the return frame. Exclusion of that obstruction forces divergent lag, made positive by inversion when necessary.
- **claim-special-ray-finite-forcing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ray-forcing)): For each fixed $s>n_0$, there is $N_s$ such that, whenever $N\geq N_s$, every line of $\Omega$ containing the directed segment $[r_s,r_N]$ contains $r_{n_0}$ and belongs to the domain of $\Theta$.
  - Explanation: Sufficiently long directed segments of the special ray force the prescribed past and membership in the fixed return domain.
- **claim-graph-magnus-basis-and-injection** (construction, [source](#stmt-mag-graph-magnus)): Let $X=\Gamma\cup_\lambda D^2$ be as above, and let $\Delta\subset\Gamma$ be a Magnus subgraph. There is a free basis $\Sigma$ of $\pi_1\Gamma$ in which $\pi_1\Delta$ is generated by a subset of $\Sigma$ omitting a letter occurring in the cyclically reduced relator. Consequently, $\pi_1\Delta\to\pi_1X$ is injective. Changing the path to the basepoint only conjugates its image.
  - Explanation: Extending a spanning tree of the Magnus subgraph gives a free basis in which its generators omit a relator letter. The Freiheitssatz then gives injectivity.
- **claim-cyclic-cover-tree-overlap-domain** (construction, [source](#stmt-mag-tree-domain)): Every connected cyclic cover of a finite connected graph has a domain $D$ for which $D\cap\tau D$ is a nonempty tree.
  - Explanation: A finite connected domain with minimal edge count among those with connected adjacent overlap has tree overlap.
- **claim-decreasing-magnus-hnn-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-mag-splitting)): Suppose $\operatorname{rank}\pi_1\Gamma\geq2$ and that $\lambda$ represents a non-power in $\pi_1\Gamma$. There are a domain $U\subset\widetilde\Gamma$ and a closed lift $\widetilde\lambda\colon S^1\to U$ with the following properties. Set $$U_-=U\cap\tau^{-1}U,\qquad U_+=U\cap\tau U, \qquad Z=U\cup_{\widetilde\lambda}D^2.$$ Both $U_-$ and $U_+$ are connected nonempty Magnus subgraphs of $Z$. Either both are trees, or $U_-\cap U_+$ is connected and nonempty. Their fundamental groups are simultaneous Magnus subgroups in a presentation of $H=\pi_1Z$, and $$\label{mag:hnn} \pi_1X\cong\langle H,t\mid tAt^{-1}=B\rangle, \qquad A=\pi_1U_-,\quad B=\pi_1U_+,$$ with edge isomorphism induced by $\tau$ and suitable basepoint paths. The vertex and edge groups inject, the lifted relator is a non-power, and $$c(U,\widetilde\lambda)<c(\Gamma,\lambda).$$
  - Explanation: A cyclic-cover domain containing a lifted relator yields an injective HNN splitting over simultaneous Magnus subgroups, with a non-power vertex relator and strictly smaller lexicographic complexity.
- **claim-nonpower-graph-hyperbolicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity)): Let $\Gamma$ be a finite connected graph, and let $\lambda\colon S^1\to\Gamma$ be a nontrivial immersed circuit representing a non-power in $\pi_1\Gamma$. If $\pi_1(\Gamma\cup_\lambda D^2)$ is $BS$-free, then it is word-hyperbolic.
  - Explanation: Induction on graph-presentation complexity proves hyperbolicity in the non-power case by verifying all restricted combination hypotheses at each splitting.
- **claim-virtual-free-by-cyclic-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-free-by-cyclic)): Let $X$ be finite, let $r\in F(X)$, and suppose that $G=F(X)/\langle\!\langle r\rangle\!\rangle$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $\operatorname{BS}(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$. There are a finite-index subgroup $H\leq G$, an integer $d\geq0$, an automorphism $\alpha$ of the rank-$d$ free group $F_d$, and an embedding $$H\hookrightarrow F_d\rtimes_\alpha\mathbb Z.$$ Consequently the same $H$ fits into an exact sequence $$1\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow1, \qquad K\text{ free},\quad Q\leq\mathbb Z.$$ Thus $G$ is virtually free-by-cyclic, with the rank of $K$ allowed to be infinite and $Q$ allowed to be trivial.
  - Explanation: Hyperbolicity, companion virtual compact specialness, and the external fibering results give a finite-index embedding into a free-by-cyclic group with finitely generated ambient free kernel. The restricted kernel may have infinite rank.

        "anchor": "stmt-ann-window-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Fix integers $\\ell,r\\geq0$. Consider the positively traversed reduced paths with edges indexed by $-\\ell,\\ldots,r$, with edge $0$ equal to $e$, and with noncyclic pointwise stabilizer. Then $$\\sum_{[W]\\in P\\backslash\\{\\text{such paths}\\}}\n       \\bar r\\bigl(\\operatorname{Stab}_G(W)\\bigr)\n \\leq\\bar r(P).\n \\label{ann:window-sum}$$ Every stabilizer in this sum is finitely generated and free.         "source_id": "PM-258.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ann-external-flaring",
        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be word-hyperbolic, let $A,B\\leq H$ be finitely generated, and let $\\phi:A\\to B$ be an isomorphism such that both attaching maps $A\\to H$ are quasi-isometric embeddings. Set $G=\\langle H,t\\mid tat^{-1}=\\phi(a),\\ a\\in A\\rangle$. Then $G$ is word-hyperbolic if there exist $\\lambda>1$ and an integer $m\\geq1$ such that, for every $\\rho>0$, there is $h(\\rho)$ for which every essential algebraic annulus as defined above, with $2m+1$ rows, width at most $\\rho$, and girth at least $h(\\rho)$, is $\\lambda$-hyperbolic.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-ann-external-flaring",

          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-ann-current-induction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\theta\\colon Q\\to F$ be an injection of nontrivial finite-rank free groups. It defines a continuous linear map $$\\theta_*\\colon\\operatorname{Curr}(Q)\\longrightarrow\n                    \\operatorname{Curr}(F)$$ which sends $\\eta_w$ to $\\eta_{\\theta(w)}$ and sends every nonzero current to a nonzero current. For the fixed bases there are constants $0<c_\\theta\\leq C_\\theta<\\infty$ such that $$c_\\theta\\|\\nu\\|_Q\\leq\\|\\theta_*\\nu\\|_F\n                     \\leq C_\\theta\\|\\nu\\|_Q\n \\qquad(\\nu\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(Q)).\n \\label{ann:induction-norm}$$ Postcomposing $\\theta$ with an inner automorphism of $F$ does not change $\\theta_*$.         "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-ann-capping",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an injection $\\theta\\colon Q\\to F$ of nontrivial finite-rank free groups with fixed bases.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ann-finite-current-window",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $G$ is not hyperbolic, and fix $m\\geq1$. There are currents $\\mu_s\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(P)$ for $-m\\leq s\\leq m$ with $$\\|\\mu_0\\|=1,\\qquad \\|\\mu_{-m}\\|,\\|\\mu_m\\|\\leq2,\n \\label{ann:finite-end-bounds}$$ and a fixed essential sign and bridge tuple such that each consecutive pair is induced by a current on its intersection subgroup. More precisely, for $$K_s=f_{\\epsilon_s}(P)\\cap\n             a_s l_{\\epsilon_{s+1}}(P)a_s^{-1}$$ there is $\\nu_s\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(K_s)$ satisfying $$(\\theta_{s,0})_*\\nu_s=\\mu_s,\n \\qquad(\\theta_{s,1})_*\\nu_s=\\mu_{s+1},\n \\label{ann:finite-compatibility}$$ where $$\\theta_{s,0}=f_{\\epsilon_s}^{-1},\\qquad\n \\theta_{s,1}=l_{\\epsilon_{s+1}}^{-1}\n                \\circ(c\\mapsto a_s^{-1}ca_s).$$ All the groups $K_s$ are nontrivial finitely generated free groups.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "anchor": "stmt-ann-trajectory",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#ann:combination\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ann:combination\">10</a>, if $G$ is not hyperbolic, there exist currents $\\mu_s\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(P)$ for $s\\in\\mathbb Z$, signs $\\epsilon_s\\in\\{+,-\\}$, and transition types $\\tau_s$ with signs $(\\epsilon_s,\\epsilon_{s+1})$, such that*\n\n1.  *$\\|\\mu_0\\|=1$, every $\\mu_s$ is nonzero, and $\\sup_{s\\in\\mathbb Z}\\|\\mu_s\\|<\\infty$;*\n\n2.  *for every $s$ there is a current $\\nu_s\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(K_{\\tau_s})$ with $$\\mu_s=(\\theta_{\\tau_s,0})_*\\nu_s,\n          \\qquad\n          \\mu_{s+1}=(\\theta_{\\tau_s,1})_*\\nu_s;$$*\n\n3.  *all types are essential: any reversing type uses a double coset outside the common side subgroup.*\n\n*The constants in <a href=\"#ann:uniform-transition-bounds\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ann:uniform-transition-bounds\">[ann:uniform-transition-bounds]</a> apply to this trajectory.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stat-finite-roots",
        "exact_quote": "There is a finite set $\\mathcal D$ of root classes with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The root of either image of a nontrivial element of a cyclic transition group belongs to $\\mathcal D$.*\n\n2.  *If a periodic axis is carried by $p\\Lambda E$ and $q\\Lambda E$ for distinct cosets $pE\\ne qE$, where $E=I$ or $E=J$, its root belongs to $\\mathcal D$.*\n\n3.  *If an axis root in a noncyclic transition group maps to a proper power under either transition injection, the root of that image belongs to $\\mathcal D$.*\n\n*Here “carried” means that both endpoints lie in the indicated translate of the limit set.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-stat-repetition",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(h_s e)$ be a reduced edge path in $\\mathcal T$, indexed by consecutive integers, and let $r_s\\in P$ be nontrivial.         "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-stat-stationary",
        "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#stat:no-atoms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"stat:no-atoms\">25</a>, there exist a nonzero current $\\nu$ on $I$ and a nonzero atomless current $\\mu$ on $P$ such that $$\\label{stat:stationary-equality}\n                         i_*\\nu=j_*\\nu=\\mu.$$ In particular, if the restricted HNN extension is not hyperbolic, Proposition <a href=\"#ann:trajectory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ann:trajectory\">21</a> supplies such a stationary current.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-stat-stationary",
          "exact_quote": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#stat:no-atoms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"stat:no-atoms\">25</a>, there exist a nonzero current $\\nu$ on $I$ and a nonzero atomless current $\\mu$ on $P$ such that $$\\label{stat:stationary-equality}\n                         i_*\\nu=j_*\\nu=\\mu.$$ In particular, if the restricted HNN extension is not hyperbolic, Proposition <a href=\"#ann:trajectory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ann:trajectory\">21</a> supplies such a stationary current.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under the assumptions of Proposition <a href=\"#stat:no-atoms\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"stat:no-atoms\">25</a>, there exist a nonzero current $\\nu$ on $I$ and a nonzero atomless current $\\mu$ on $P$ such that $$\\label{stat:stationary-equality}\n                         i_*\\nu=j_*\\nu=\\mu.$$ In particular, if the restricted HNN extension is not hyperbolic, Proposition <a href=\"#ann:trajectory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ann:trajectory\">21</a> supplies such a stationary current.",

        "anchor": "stmt-stat-transfers",
        "exact_quote": "There is a translation- and flip-invariant conull Borel set $\\mathcal U\\subset\\Omega$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Every line of $\\mathcal U$ has two irrational endpoints and is carried by a unique $I$ coset and a unique $J$ coset.*\n\n2.  *On lines carried by $p\\Lambda I$, translate by $p^{-1}$ and apply the boundary isomorphism from $I$ to $J$. This forward transfer, and the analogous backward transfer from $q\\Lambda J$, preserve $\\mu$ on their domains.*\n\n3.  *Every defined transfer, its inverse, and every output frame translation take lines of $\\mathcal U$ to lines of $\\mathcal U$. Consequently all successive forward and backward transfers exist on $\\mathcal U$.*\n\n*Measure preservation in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> holds on the full Borel domain of lines with irrational endpoints, not only after restriction to $\\mathcal U$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-stat-windows",
        "exact_quote": "Every line $L\\in\\mathcal U$ determines an actual aligned bilateral path of positively traversed edges $$\\cdots,E_{-1},E_0=e,E_1,\\cdots$$ in $\\mathcal T$, with transferred lines in its successive edge frames. In particular, an irrational point can belong to the limit sets of at most one such window stabilizer.*\n\n4.  *If an aligned one-sided window joins $e$ to $he$, its stabilizer is $S=P\\cap hPh^{-1}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-car-return",
        "exact_quote": "The map $F$ is an order-preserving bijection with order-preserving inverse. It permutes the finite set of maximal classes of $\\mathcal C$. For every representative $M$ of such a class, there are an integer $p\\ge1$ and an element $v\\in G$ of height $p$ such that $$v^{-1}Mv=M.$$ The automorphism $\\alpha:M\\to M$, $\\alpha(m)=v^{-1}mv$, is atoroidal: no nontrivial conjugacy class in $M$ is periodic under $\\alpha$.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-car-return",
          "exact_quote": "The map $F$ is an order-preserving bijection with order-preserving inverse. It permutes the finite set of maximal classes of $\\mathcal C$. For every representative $M$ of such a class, there are an integer $p\\ge1$ and an element $v\\in G$ of height $p$ such that $$v^{-1}Mv=M.$$ The automorphism $\\alpha:M\\to M$, $\\alpha(m)=v^{-1}mv$, is atoroidal: no nontrivial conjugacy class in $M$ is periodic under $\\alpha$.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The map $F$ is an order-preserving bijection with order-preserving inverse. It permutes the finite set of maximal classes of $\\mathcal C$.       "explanation": "Forward transfer is an order automorphism on carrier classes. A return to a maximal class induces an onto atoroidal automorphism, with atoroidality forced by Baumslag–Solitar exclusion.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-car-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M,v,\\alpha$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#car:return\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"car:return\">36</a>, and choose a free basis of $M$. There exist $b\\ge1$, an automorphism $\\beta=\\alpha^b$, and $\\lambda>4$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lambda|w|_M\n &\\le\\max\\{|\\beta(w)|_M,|\\beta^{-1}(w)|_M\\},\n &&w\\in M,                                      \\label{car:based}\\\\\n \\lambda\\ell_M(w)\n &\\le\\max\\{\\ell_M(\\beta(w)),\\ell_M(\\beta^{-1}(w))\\},\n &&w\\in M.                                      \\label{car:cyclic}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every $M$-current $\\sigma$ one has $$\\lambda\\|\\sigma\\|_M\n \\le\\|\\beta_*\\sigma\\|_M+\n       \\|(\\beta^{-1})_*\\sigma\\|_M.\n \\label{car:current}$$*\n\n*The partial conjugations along the paths to $v^be$ and $v^{-b}e$ have domains $$S_+=P\\cap v^bPv^{-b},\\qquad\n S_-=P\\cap v^{-b}Pv^b,$$ respectively. These are finitely generated full window stabilizers, and both contain $M$. Their boundary transfers are defined on all of $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_+$ and $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_-$. The transfers $\\Theta_+,\\Theta_-$ restrict on $\\partial^2\\Lambda M$ to $\\beta,\\beta^{-1}$, respectively, and preserve $\\mu$ on their irrational-endpoint domains.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"

          "exact_quote": "Let $M,v,\\alpha$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#car:return\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"car:return\">36</a>, and choose a free basis of $M$. These are finitely generated full window stabilizers, and both contain $M$. Their boundary transfers are defined on all of $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_+$ and $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_-$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-exp-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Let $a:Q\\to F$ and $b:Q\\to F'$ be injective homomorphisms between finitely generated free groups with fixed bases. For every $D\\ge0$ there are constants $E,T\\ge0$ with the following properties. Let $L$ be an oriented line of $\\mathcal C_Q$, and let $L_a,L_b$ be its tightened image lines in $\\mathcal C_F,\\mathcal C_{F'}$. If $q\\in Q$ satisfies $d(a(q),L_a)\\le D$, then $$d(b(q),L_b)\\le E.$$ If two such elements $q_1,q_2$ have projections to $L_a$ positively ordered and separated by more than $T$, then their projections to $L_b$ are in the same order. As their separation on $L_a$ tends to infinity, their separation on $L_b$ tends to infinity, uniformly over all $L,q_1,q_2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-258.1"
      },

As their separation on $L_a$ tends to infinity, their separation on $L_b$ tends to infinity, uniformly over all $L,q_1,q_2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-exp-trimming",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a maximal carrier $M$ and the maps above. There are constants $Q\\ge0$ and $L_*>0$ with the following property. Let $L$ be an oriented $M$-Cayley-tree line, let $x,y$ be vertices on $L$ with $d_M(x,y)=l\\ge L_*$, and let $\\mathcal A\\subset\\mathcal U$ be a Borel set whose lines lie within $Q$ of both $x,y$ in $\\mathcal C_P$. Suppose a sign $\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x,\\beta^\\epsilon y)\\ge\\lambda l.$$ If every line of $\\mathcal A$ belongs to $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_\\epsilon$, then its image $\\mathcal A'=\\Theta_\\epsilon(\\mathcal A)$ has the same measure, lies in $\\mathcal U$, and admits vertices $x',y'$ on the tightened line $\\beta^\\epsilon L$ such that $$d_P(x',\\ell),d_P(y',\\ell)\\le Q\\quad(\\ell\\in\\mathcal A'),\n \\qquad d_M(x',y')\\ge2l.$$ The same sign satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x',\\beta^\\epsilon y')\n \\ge\\lambda d_M(x',y').$$ The constants are independent of $L,x,y,\\mathcal A$ and of the number of times this assertion is applied.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix a maximal carrier $M$ and the maps above. There are constants $Q\\ge0$ and $L_*>0$ with the following property.         "anchor": "stmt-exp-finite-failure",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $L_0\\ge L_*$, $c>0$, $C_2\\ge0$, and an integer $D\\ge1$, depending only on the fixed splitting, carrier, and return maps, with the following property. Suppose $\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x_0,\\beta^\\epsilon y_0)\\ge\\lambda l_0.$$ Then there are $n\\ge0$, a line $\\ell\\in\\mathcal U$, and an oriented periodic $P$-line $\\zeta$ of primitive period at most $D$ such that $\\ell$, $\\zeta$, and the tightened $P$-image $\\eta_n$ of $\\beta^{\\epsilon n}L$ share a segment of length at least $$c\\,2^n l_0-C_2.\n \\label{exp:failure-overlap}$$ The endpoints of $\\zeta$ lie in the limit sets of the stabilizers of two distinct actual windows of the shape defining $\\Theta_\\epsilon$.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\psi$ be an automorphism of a finitely generated free group $M$ with a fixed basis. There is an integer $C_4\\ge0$, depending only on $\\psi$, with the following property. Suppose two lines $L_1,L_2$ of $\\mathcal C_M$ share a segment $[u,z^k u]$, where $u$ is a vertex, $z$ translates $L_2$, and $k>C_4$.         "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\operatorname{rank}\\pi_1\\Gamma\\geq2$ and that $\\lambda$ represents a non-power in $\\pi_1\\Gamma$. There are a domain $U\\subset\\widetilde\\Gamma$ and a closed lift $\\widetilde\\lambda\\colon S^1\\to U$ with the following properties. Set $$U_-=U\\cap\\tau^{-1}U,\\qquad U_+=U\\cap\\tau U,\n \\qquad Z=U\\cup_{\\widetilde\\lambda}D^2.$$ Both $U_-$ and $U_+$ are connected nonempty Magnus subgraphs of $Z$. Either both are trees, or $U_-\\cap U_+$ is connected and nonempty. Their fundamental groups are simultaneous Magnus subgroups in a presentation of $H=\\pi_1Z$, and $$\\label{mag:hnn}\n \\pi_1X\\cong\\langle H,t\\mid tAt^{-1}=B\\rangle,\n \\qquad A=\\pi_1U_-,\\quad B=\\pi_1U_+,$$ with edge isomorphism induced by $\\tau$ and suitable basepoint paths. The vertex and edge groups inject, the lifted relator is a non-power, and $$c(U,\\widetilde\\lambda)<c(\\Gamma,\\lambda).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-free-by-cyclic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be finite, let $r\\in F(X)$, and suppose that $G=F(X)/\\langle\\!\\langle r\\rangle\\!\\rangle$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $\\operatorname{BS}(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$. There are a finite-index subgroup $H\\leq G$, an integer $d\\geq0$, an automorphism $\\alpha$ of the rank-$d$ free group $F_d$, and an embedding $$H\\hookrightarrow F_d\\rtimes_\\alpha\\mathbb Z.$$ Consequently the same $H$ fits into an exact sequence $$1\\longrightarrow K\\longrightarrow H\\longrightarrow Q\\longrightarrow1,\n\\qquad K\\text{ free},\\quad Q\\leq\\mathbb Z.$$ Thus $G$ is virtually free-by-cyclic, with the rank of $K$ allowed to be infinite and $Q$ allowed to be trivial.",

<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Baumslag–Solitar group** ([source](#pm-h-2)): The Baumslag–Solitar groups $$\operatorname{BS}(m,n) =\langle a,t\mid ta^mt^{-1}=a^n\rangle, \qquad m,n\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\},$$ are the basic obstructions to hyperbolicity among one-relator groups.
- **Baumslag–Solitar-free** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We call a group with this exclusion *Baumslag–Solitar-free*.
- **Subgroup-closed free-by-cyclic** ([source](#pm-h-2)): We use *free-by-cyclic* in the subgroup-closed sense: a group $H$ fits into an exact sequence $$1\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow1$$ with $K$ free of possibly infinite rank and $Q\leq\mathbb Z$, possibly trivial.
- **Primitivity rank** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Here the primitivity rank of a relator $r$ is the least rank of a subgroup of $F(X)$ containing $r$ in which $r$ is not part of a free basis, with value $\infty$ if no such subgroup exists.
- **Local quasiconvexity** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Local quasiconvexity means that every finitely generated subgroup is quasiconvex.
- **Immersed circuit** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A *circuit* in a graph is a map from a subdivided circle; it is *immersed* when its edge path has no backtracking, including at the closing point.
- **Non-power** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A nontrivial element is a *non-power* if it is not $u^k$ with $\|k\|\geq2$.
- **Word-hyperbolicity** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A group is *word-hyperbolic* if geodesic triangles in a Cayley graph are uniformly thin: each side lies in a fixed neighborhood of the other two.
- **Quasiconvex subgroup** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A subgroup of a hyperbolic group is *quasiconvex* if every geodesic between its elements lies in a fixed neighborhood of the subgroup.
- **Boundary limit set** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a subset $Q$ of a hyperbolic group, write $\Lambda Q$ for its accumulation set in the boundary.
- **Commensurable subgroups** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Two subgroups are *commensurable* if their intersection has finite index in both.
- **Subgroup commensurator** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a subgroup $C\leq H$, its commensurator is $$\operatorname{Comm}_H(C) =\{h\in H: C\text{ and }hCh^{-1}\text{ are commensurable}\}.$$
- **Reduced rank** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a finitely generated free group $K$, put $$\bar r(K)=\max\{\operatorname{rk}(K)-1,0\}.$$
- **Strong inertia** ([source](#stmt-pre-strong-inertia-definition)): A finitely generated subgroup $E$ of a finitely generated free group $P$ is *strongly inert* if, for every finitely generated subgroup $S\leq P$, $$\sum_{SpE\in S\backslash P/E} \bar r(E\cap p^{-1}Sp)\leq\bar r(S). \label{pre:strong-inertia-inequality}$$
- **Edge frame** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *frame* for the edge $he$ is the element $h\in G$; changing $h$ to $hp$, with $p\in P$, changes the coordinates on its stabilizer but not the edge.
- **Algebraic annulus** ([source](#ann:row-equation)): An *algebraic annulus with $2m+1$ rows* consists of signs $\epsilon_s\in\{+,-\}$ and elements $c_s\in P\setminus\{1\}$ for $-m\leq s\leq m$, together with bridges $a_s\in H$ for $-m\leq s<m$, satisfying $$f_{\epsilon_s}(c_s) =a_s l_{\epsilon_{s+1}}(c_{s+1})a_s^{-1}. \label{ann:row-equation}$$
- **Annular width and girth** ([source](#ann:row-equation)): Its width is $\max_s\|a_s\|_H$ and its girth is $\|c_0\|_P$.
- **Essential annulus** ([source](#ann:row-equation)): It is *essential* if a $+,-$ transition has $a_s\notin A$ and a $-,+$ transition has $a_s\notin B$.
- **Free-group current** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A *current* on $F$ is a nonnegative, $F$-invariant, flip-invariant Radon measure on $\partial^2F$.
- **Counting current** ([source](#ann:counting-norm)): For $w\in F\setminus\{1\}$, the *counting current* $\eta_w$ is the sum of point masses at the two orientations of the translates of the axis of $w$, indexed by $F/\langle w\rangle$.
- **Transition types** ([source](#ann:type-maps)): Call the resulting finite set of triples $\tau=(\epsilon,\delta,d)$ the *transition types*, and write $$K_\tau=K_{\epsilon,\delta,d},\qquad \theta_{\tau,0}=f_\epsilon^{-1},\qquad \theta_{\tau,1}=l_\delta^{-1}\circ(c\mapsto d^{-1}cd). \label{ann:type-maps}$$
- **Axis root** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A *root of an axis* in the Cayley tree of $P$ means a generator of its stabilizer in $P$.
- **Rational boundary point** ([source](#pm-h-20)): A boundary point is *rational* if a nontrivial element of the free group fixes it.
- **Unbased subgroup core** ([source](#pm-h-23)): For a finitely generated nontrivial subgroup $K\le P$, put $$C(K)=K\backslash\operatorname{Hull}_{T_P}(\Lambda K).$$
- **Natural edge** ([source](#pm-h-23)): A *natural edge* is a maximal nonbacktracking path whose interior vertices have degree two.
- **Small graph** ([source](#stmt-car-small-graph)): The *small graph* $\Gamma^{\mathrm{sm}}$ consists of the natural vertices and the natural edges with fixed labels, subdivided according to those labels.
- **Left-special occurring word** ([source](#pm-h-34)): An occurring word $\rho$ is *left-special* if there are distinct basis letters $a,b$ such that both $a\rho$ and $b\rho$ occur.
- **Magnus subgraph** ([source](#pm-h-38)): We call a connected subgraph $\Delta\subset\Gamma$ a *Magnus subgraph* if $S_\lambda\not\subset\Delta$; equivalently, $\Delta$ omits an edge traversed by $\lambda$.
- **Cyclic-cover domain** ([source](#pm-h-39)): A *domain* will mean a finite connected subgraph $D\subset\widetilde\Gamma$ such that the occurrences of every base vertex and every base open edge form a nonempty interval of translates.
- **Magnus induction complexity** ([source](#mag:complexity)): The construction also accounts for strict decrease of the complexity $$\label{mag:complexity} c(\Gamma,\lambda)= \bigl(\|\lambda\|-\|V(S_\lambda)\|, \operatorname{rank}\pi_1\Gamma\bigr),$$ ordered lexicographically.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-reduced-rank | \bar r(K) | = | $\max\{\operatorname{rk}(K)-1,0\}$ | The rank is the free-group rank. |
| q-fixed-window-total-reduced-rank | Total reduced rank of noncyclic fixed-shape window stabilizers | ≤ | $\bar r(P)$ | The shape is fixed; the estimate is not a bound over all lengths. |
| q-fixed-window-orbit-count | Number of noncyclic window orbits | ≤ | $\bar r(P)$ | Orbits are taken under the edge stabilizer with distinguished edge fixed. |
| q-current-norm | \\|\mu\\|_F | = | $\frac12\mu\bigl(\mathcal L_F(1)\bigr)$ | The norm uses the oriented lines through the identity. |
| q-counting-current-norm | \\|\eta_w\\|_F | = | $\|w\|_{F,\mathrm{cyc}}$ | Proper-power multiplicity is retained by the stated coset indexing. |
| q-capping-frequency-error | Difference between induced counting-current cylinder mass and symmetrized linear occurrences | ≤ | $D_{\theta,u}$ | The error depends on the injection and test word, but not on the capped source word. |
| q-capping-length-error | Difference between capped induced-current norm and based image length | ≤ | $D_\theta$ | The constant is independent of the source word. |
| q-finite-window-central-norm | \\|\mu_0\\| | = | $1$ | Normalization uses the original central based girth. |
| q-finite-window-end-norms | Norm of either end current | ≤ | $2$ | The bound concerns the end rows, not every intermediate row. |
| q-adjacent-current-upper-comparison | \\|\mu_{s+1}\\| | ≤ | $C\\|\mu_s\\|$ | The comparison constant is independent of the window length. |
| q-adjacent-current-lower-comparison | \\|\mu_{s+1}\\| | ≥ | $C^{-1}\\|\mu_s\\|$ | The lower comparison retains nonzero mass in bilateral limits. |
| q-transition-current-norm | \\|\nu_s\\| | ≤ | $D\\|\mu_s\\|$ | The constant is uniform over the finite type list and independent of window length. |
| q-bilateral-current-nonvanishing | \\|\mu_s\\| | ≥ | $C^{-\|s\|}$ | The index ranges over the bilateral trajectory. |
| q-natural-core-edge-count | Unoriented edges after suppressing degree-two interiors | ≤ | $3r_0$ | The bound controls natural edges, not subdivided label lengths. |
| q-natural-core-vertex-count | Vertices after suppressing degree-two interiors | ≤ | $2r_0$ | The same bound applies to each fixed positive window shape. |
| q-core-circuit-length | Length of an immersed circuit containing a specified edge | ≤ | $2\|E(C)\|$ | Edges have unit length and the edge count is unoriented. |
| q-return-expansion-factor | \lambda | > | $4$ | The basis and return power are fixed before subsequent transport. |
| q-trimmed-segment-growth | Distance between the trimmed output vertices | ≥ | $2l$ | The segment must exceed the fixed threshold; trimming constants are independent of iteration. |
| q-failure-shared-segment | Shared segment length at finite return failure | ≥ | $c\,2^n l_0-C_2$ | Constants depend only on the fixed splitting, carrier, and return maps. |
| q-retained-whole-periods | Whole image periods in the shared tightened segment | ≥ | $k-C_4$ | The loss is additive in whole periods rather than metric length. |
| q-carrier-overlap-threshold | L_D | = | $(V+1)D$ | The vertex count and ambient primitive-period bound determine the threshold. |
| q-carrier-primitive-cyclic-length | \ell_M(z) | ≤ | $AVD$ | The primitive carrier translation may be a proper power of the primitive ambient translation. |

          "anchor": "stmt-car-expansion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M,v,\\alpha$ be as in Proposition <a href=\"#car:return\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"car:return\">36</a>, and choose a free basis of $M$. There exist $b\\ge1$, an automorphism $\\beta=\\alpha^b$, and $\\lambda>4$ such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\lambda|w|_M\n &\\le\\max\\{|\\beta(w)|_M,|\\beta^{-1}(w)|_M\\},\n &&w\\in M,                                      \\label{car:based}\\\\\n \\lambda\\ell_M(w)\n &\\le\\max\\{\\ell_M(\\beta(w)),\\ell_M(\\beta^{-1}(w))\\},\n &&w\\in M.                                      \\label{car:cyclic}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For every $M$-current $\\sigma$ one has $$\\lambda\\|\\sigma\\|_M\n \\le\\|\\beta_*\\sigma\\|_M+\n       \\|(\\beta^{-1})_*\\sigma\\|_M.\n \\label{car:current}$$*\n\n*The partial conjugations along the paths to $v^be$ and $v^{-b}e$ have domains $$S_+=P\\cap v^bPv^{-b},\\qquad\n S_-=P\\cap v^{-b}Pv^b,$$ respectively. These are finitely generated full window stabilizers, and both contain $M$. Their boundary transfers are defined on all of $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_+$ and $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_-$. The transfers $\\Theta_+,\\Theta_-$ restrict on $\\partial^2\\Lambda M$ to $\\beta,\\beta^{-1}$, respectively, and preserve $\\mu$ on their irrational-endpoint domains.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-exp-trimming",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a maximal carrier $M$ and the maps above. There are constants $Q\\ge0$ and $L_*>0$ with the following property. Let $L$ be an oriented $M$-Cayley-tree line, let $x,y$ be vertices on $L$ with $d_M(x,y)=l\\ge L_*$, and let $\\mathcal A\\subset\\mathcal U$ be a Borel set whose lines lie within $Q$ of both $x,y$ in $\\mathcal C_P$. Suppose a sign $\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x,\\beta^\\epsilon y)\\ge\\lambda l.$$ If every line of $\\mathcal A$ belongs to $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_\\epsilon$, then its image $\\mathcal A'=\\Theta_\\epsilon(\\mathcal A)$ has the same measure, lies in $\\mathcal U$, and admits vertices $x',y'$ on the tightened line $\\beta^\\epsilon L$ such that $$d_P(x',\\ell),d_P(y',\\ell)\\le Q\\quad(\\ell\\in\\mathcal A'),\n \\qquad d_M(x',y')\\ge2l.$$ The same sign satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x',\\beta^\\epsilon y')\n \\ge\\lambda d_M(x',y').$$ The constants are independent of $L,x,y,\\mathcal A$ and of the number of times this assertion is applied.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "There are constants $L_0\\ge L_*$, $c>0$, $C_2\\ge0$, and an integer $D\\ge1$, depending only on the fixed splitting, carrier, and return maps, with the following property. Let $\\eta\\in\\Omega\\cap\\partial^2\\Lambda M$, let $L$ be its corresponding oriented $M$-Cayley-tree line, and choose vertices $x_0<y_0$ on $L$ with $l_0=d_M(x_0,y_0)\\ge L_0$. Suppose $\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x_0,\\beta^\\epsilon y_0)\\ge\\lambda l_0.$$ Then there are $n\\ge0$, a line $\\ell\\in\\mathcal U$, and an oriented periodic $P$-line $\\zeta$ of primitive period at most $D$ such that $\\ell$, $\\zeta$, and the tightened $P$-image $\\eta_n$ of $\\beta^{\\epsilon n}L$ share a segment of length at least $$c\\,2^n l_0-C_2.\n \\label{exp:failure-overlap}$$ The endpoints of $\\zeta$ lie in the limit sets of the stabilizers of two distinct actual windows of the shape defining $\\Theta_\\epsilon$. One is the prescribed window, with stabilizer $S_\\epsilon$.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }

            "exact_quote": "There are constants $L_0\\ge L_*$, $c>0$, $C_2\\ge0$, and an integer $D\\ge1$, depending only on the fixed splitting, carrier, and return maps, with the following property.",
