<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-quasiconvex-intersections** [claim](#stmt-pre-quasiconvex-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-quasiconvex-intersection): Bound intersections of subgroup neighborhoods and pass to boundary rays. -> Quasiconvex finite intersections with the stated limit-set identity.
- **step-double-coset-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-pre-double-coset-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-double-coset-finiteness): Use the axis endpoints of an infinite-order intersection element to find a bounded double-coset representative. -> Finitely many relevant double cosets.
- **step-cyclic-commensurator** [claim](#stmt-pre-cyclic-commensurator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-cyclic-commensurator): Convert the finite double-coset list into finitely many cyclic-subgroup cosets. -> A virtually cyclic commensurator, cyclic under torsion-freeness.
- **step-cyclic-tree-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-pre-gbs-subgroup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-gbs-subgroup): Reduce a finite graph of infinite cyclic groups and apply graph-of-groups normal form. -> An embedded Baumslag–Solitar subgroup.
- **step-elliptic-commensuration** [claim](#stmt-pre-elliptic-commensurator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-elliptic-commensurator): Restrict to the orbit hull and use cyclic commensurators to identify its stabilizers. -> A cyclic-stabilizer tree action giving the obstruction.
- **step-off-diagonal-inertia** [claim](#stmt-pre-cyclic-off-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-cyclic-off-diagonal): Apply the strong-inertia inequality with the subgroup itself as test subgroup. -> Trivial or cyclic off-diagonal self-intersections.
- **step-frames** [claim](#stmt-ann-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-frames): Use the double-coset overlap hypothesis to classify neighbors and prohibit traversal reversals. -> Aligned frame formulas and coordinate transfers.
- **step-window-rank-budget** [claim](#stmt-ann-window-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-window-bound): Extend windows one edge at a time and sum the separately assumed inertia inequalities over extension orbits. -> The fixed-shape reduced-rank and orbit bounds.
- **step-distinct-window-overlap** [claim](#stmt-ann-distinct-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-distinct-paths): Compare the first divergent overlap cosets. -> At most cyclic common stabilizer for distinct paths.
- **step-external-flaring** [claim](#stmt-ann-external-flaring) / [proof](#pm-h-13): Invoke the external algebraic annular-flaring criterion with its stated quantifier order. -> A sufficient annular condition for hyperbolicity.
- **step-counterannuli** [claim](#stmt-ann-counterannuli) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-counterannuli): Negate flaring at a fixed row count and extract fixed signs and bridges. -> Counterannuli with diverging central girth and bounded row ratios.
- **step-current-compactness** [claim](#stmt-ann-current-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-current-compactness): Diagonalize cylinder coordinates and retain the finite cylinder extension identities. -> Compactness in the current topology.
- **step-current-induction** [claim](#stmt-ann-current-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-current-induction): Induce boundary measures over all target cosets and use finite subgroup cores for local finiteness. -> Continuous functorial current induction with positive norm comparisons.
- **step-word-capping** [claim](#stmt-ann-capping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-capping): Choose a short cap in the source and bound cancellation and conjugating tails after injection. -> Uniform end-error estimates for length and cylinder frequencies.
- **step-finite-current-window** [claim](#stmt-ann-finite-current-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-finite-current-window): Normalize capped transition counting currents by central girth and take fixed-window limits. -> Compatible finite row-current windows.
- **step-finite-transition-types** [claim](#stmt-ann-finite-types) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-finite-types): Replace fixed bridges by double-coset representatives after current limits and remove inner conjugations. -> Uniform norm comparisons from a finite transition-type list.
- **step-bilateral-current-trajectory** [claim](#stmt-ann-trajectory) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ann-trajectory): Take diagonal window limits, recentering and renormalizing at a maximal row when necessary. -> A bounded nonzero bilateral current trajectory.
- **step-atomic-axis** [claim](#stmt-stat-atomic-axis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-atomic-axis): Translate an atomic line through the identity and use local finiteness to force repeated translates. -> A periodic axis and its norm contribution.
- **step-exceptional-roots** [claim](#stmt-stat-finite-roots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-finite-roots): Collect roots from cyclic types, off-diagonal intersections, and proper-power image ambiguities. -> A finite exceptional root dictionary.
- **step-root-repetition** [claim](#stmt-stat-repetition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-repetition): Concatenate translated repeated path segments to obtain a tree-hyperbolic commensurator. -> A Baumslag–Solitar subgroup.
- **step-remove-atoms** [claim](#stmt-stat-no-atoms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-no-atoms): Propagate a chosen atom along actual essential paths and force root repetition. -> Atomless row currents.
- **step-stationary-current** [claim](#stmt-stat-stationary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-stationary): Use normalized telescoping averages or a summable positive bilateral series, then re-establish atomlessness. -> A nonzero stationary atomless current and transition current.
- **step-irrational-endpoints** [claim](#stmt-stat-irrational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-irrational): Use disjoint translates near rational endpoints and subgroup-core recurrence for reflection. -> Almost-everywhere irrationality and rationality preservation and reflection.
- **step-conull-transfers** [claim](#stmt-stat-transfers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-transfers): Use unique irrational carriers in the coset induction formulas and saturate null exceptions under all partial operations. -> An invariant conull Borel set with measure-preserving transfers.
- **step-actual-windows** [claim](#stmt-stat-windows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-windows): Develop unique carrier cosets into actual edges and intersect stabilizers along finite windows. -> Unique aligned bilateral paths with noncyclic finite-window stabilizers.
- **step-local-window-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-stat-window-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-stat-window-finiteness): Combine finitely many window orbits with finitely many subgroup-core states meeting the chosen ball. -> Local finiteness of actual windows.
- **step-window-cores** [claim](#stmt-car-window-cores) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-window-cores): Suppress degree-two interiors, interpret core states as actual windows, and extend conull liftability by closedness. -> Bounded natural graph complexity and lifts of every support line.
- **step-small-graph** [claim](#stmt-car-small-graph) / [proof](#stmt-car-small-graph): Select a fixed abstract natural graph type, fixed bounded labels, and convergent prefixes of divergent labels. -> A fixed labeled small graph along a subsequence.
- **step-conjugacy-partial-order** [claim](#stmt-car-conjugate-containment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-conjugate-containment): A proper self-conjugate inclusion would force infinitely many distinct Schreier states close to a finite core. -> Antisymmetry of conjugacy containment.
- **step-bounded-circuits** [claim](#stmt-car-circuits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-circuits): Use a simple cycle for a nonbridge and join circuits on both sides of a bridge. -> A bounded immersed circuit through each edge.
- **step-finite-carriers** [claim](#stmt-car-finite-carriers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-finite-carriers): Short circuits prevent a fixed subgroup core from traversing divergent natural edges. -> Finite cofinal carriers and finitely many maximal classes.
- **step-carrier-return** [claim](#stmt-car-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-return): Shift carrier coordinates, return through the finite maximal-class permutation, and exclude periodic conjugacy by height. -> Onto atoroidal carrier return automorphisms.
- **step-return-expansion** [claim](#stmt-car-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-expansion): Amplify external atoroidal expansion, pass to cyclic lengths, and approximate currents by cycle circulations. -> Based, cyclic, and sum-form current expansion.
- **step-zero-carrier-mass** [claim](#stmt-car-zero-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-car-zero-mass): Restrict the stationary measure to a carrier boundary and compare invariance with expansion. -> Zero current mass on each maximal carrier and its translates.
- **step-transfer-geometry** [claim](#stmt-exp-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-transport): Compare source-line vertices and projections using fixed quasi-isometric embedding bounds. -> Uniform nearness, eventual order, and divergent separation under transfer.
- **step-expanding-trimming** [claim](#stmt-exp-trimming) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-trimming): Delete fixed end portions after transfer and rule out the opposite expanding direction at the trimmed pair. -> Repeatable growth with fixed nearness and preserved mass.
- **step-finite-return-failure** [claim](#stmt-exp-finite-failure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-finite-failure): Exclude indefinite positive-mass concentration on a zero-mass carrier and analyze the competing-window core fiber product. -> A finite failure with a long bounded-period common segment.
- **step-carrier-support-periodicity** [claim](#stmt-exp-carrier-periodic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-carrier-periodic): Recenter increasingly long failure overlaps and fix a bounded-period axis along a subsequence. -> A periodic line in the closed support.
- **step-whole-period-pullback** [claim](#stmt-exp-period-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-period-pullback): Use equivariant projections to trim a bounded number of whole image periods without phase loss. -> Bounded additive loss of whole periods.
- **step-periodic-label-agreement** [claim](#stmt-exp-periodic-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-periodic-labels): Compare periodic patterns using addition modulo a period and their common divisor. -> Global label agreement and equality of actual oriented lines.
- **step-carrier-period-bound** [claim](#stmt-exp-carrier-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-carrier-period): Repeat a period phase in the finite carrier core and compare cyclic lengths through the subgroup embedding. -> Carrier membership of the axis and bounded primitive carrier period.
- **step-no-periodic-support** [claim](#stmt-exp-no-periodic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-no-periodic): Build a periodic support trajectory, locate a late unambiguous carrier return, and pull a failure axis back by counting whole periods. -> Exclusion of periodic support lines.
- **step-entire-support** [claim](#stmt-exp-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-support): Exclude all small-component support carriers, use compactness for bounded small segments, and extend transfers by finite-window uniqueness. -> Irrational endpoints and unique compatible transfers on the entire support.
- **step-special-rays** [claim](#stmt-ray-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ray-finite): Count natural-vertex prefixes followed by limiting long-edge labels; exclude absence of branching with a finite language graph. -> A finite nonempty set of left-special occurring words.
- **step-positive-lag** [claim](#stmt-ray-lag) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ray-lag): Return on a finite endpoint orbit and exclude bounded lag by a commuting nontrivial element of zero height. -> Divergent positive lag after possible inversion.
- **step-finite-ray-forcing** [claim](#stmt-ray-forcing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ray-forcing): Use compactness for unique pasts and local window finiteness for the fixed transfer domain. -> Finite cylinder conditions forcing the past and return domain.
- **step-combination-mass-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-ann-combination) / [proof](#pm-h-36): Use positive lag to nest equal-mass cylinders, then translate their positive-mass intersection into disjoint subsets of a compact line set. -> Contradiction to nonhyperbolicity and completion of the restricted combination theorem.
- **step-graph-magnus** [claim](#stmt-mag-graph-magnus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mag-graph-magnus): Extend a subgraph spanning tree and verify that the reduced relator uses an omitted basis letter. -> A generator-subset Magnus subgroup with injective image.
- **step-tree-overlap-domain** [claim](#stmt-mag-tree-domain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mag-tree-domain): Construct large connected level intervals and minimize the number of domain edges while keeping connected adjacent overlap. -> A domain with nonempty tree overlap.
- **step-magnus-splitting** [claim](#stmt-mag-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mag-splitting): Separate the lifted-support projection cases, build the relator-containing domain, reconstruct the HNN group, and choose simultaneous overlap bases. -> An injective HNN splitting with strictly smaller vertex complexity.
- **step-nonpower-induction** [claim](#stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity): Induct on lexicographic complexity and verify torsion-freeness, Magnus quasiconvexity, conjugate-intersection restrictions, and both inertia inequalities. -> Hyperbolicity for Baumslag–Solitar-free non-power graph presentations.
- **step-main-relator-reductions** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity-2): Reduce the relator, separate the trivial and proper-power cases, and apply graph induction in the non-power case. -> The main hyperbolicity theorem for all relators under the stated subgroup exclusion.
- **step-magnus-quasiconvexity** [claim](#stmt-thm-magnus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity-3): Combine graph-to-basis injectivity with ambient hyperbolicity and the external Magnus quasiconvexity theorem. -> Injective quasiconvex Magnus subgraph images.
- **step-virtual-free-by-cyclic** [claim](#stmt-cor-free-by-cyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-free-by-cyclic): Apply companion virtual compact specialness and external fibering results, then restrict the ambient cyclic projection. -> The finite-index semidirect-product embedding and subgroup-closed free-by-cyclic exact sequence.

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ann-frames",
          "exact_quote": "A reduced path in $\\mathcal T$ with noncyclic pointwise stabilizer has constant traversal sign. The positive successors of $e$ that can occur in such a path have the form $pte$, with $pI\\in P/I$, and the positive predecessors have the form $pt^{-1}e$, with $pJ\\in P/J$. Their common stabilizers with $e$ are, respectively, $$pIp^{-1}\\quad\\text{and}\\quad pJp^{-1}.$$ For a positive successor the coordinate transfer is $c\\mapsto t^{-1}p^{-1}cpt$; for a positive predecessor it is $c\\mapsto tp^{-1}cpt^{-1}$, on the indicated domains.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-frames",

      ],

      "output": "Aligned frame formulas and coordinate transfers.",

          "anchor": "stmt-ann-window-bound",
          "exact_quote": "Fix integers $\\ell,r\\geq0$. Then $$\\sum_{[W]\\in P\\backslash\\{\\text{such paths}\\}}\n       \\bar r\\bigl(\\operatorname{Stab}_G(W)\\bigr)\n \\leq\\bar r(P).\n \\label{ann:window-sum}$$ Every stabilizer in this sum is finitely generated and free. In particular, there are at most $\\bar r(P)$ such $P$-orbits. Equivalently, the same bound holds for $G$-orbits if the distinguished edge is allowed to vary.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-ann-external-flaring",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be word-hyperbolic, let $A,B\\leq H$ be finitely generated, and let $\\phi:A\\to B$ be an isomorphism such that both attaching maps $A\\to H$ are quasi-isometric embeddings. Set $G=\\langle H,t\\mid tat^{-1}=\\phi(a),\\ a\\in A\\rangle$. Then $G$ is word-hyperbolic if there exist $\\lambda>1$ and an integer $m\\geq1$ such that, for every $\\rho>0$, there is $h(\\rho)$ for which every essential algebraic annulus as defined above, with $2m+1$ rows, width at most $\\rho$, and girth at least $h(\\rho)$, is $\\lambda$-hyperbolic.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-external-flaring",
      "inputs": [
        "Hyperbolic vertex group",
        "Quasi-isometrically embedded attaching maps"
      ],
      "method": "Invoke the external algebraic annular-flaring criterion with its stated quantifier order.",

The bounds and the fixed bridge tuple may depend on $m$.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-ann-current-induction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\theta\\colon Q\\to F$ be an injection of nontrivial finite-rank free groups. It defines a continuous linear map $$\\theta_*\\colon\\operatorname{Curr}(Q)\\longrightarrow\n                    \\operatorname{Curr}(F)$$ which sends $\\eta_w$ to $\\eta_{\\theta(w)}$ and sends every nonzero current to a nonzero current. For the fixed bases there are constants $0<c_\\theta\\leq C_\\theta<\\infty$ such that $$c_\\theta\\|\\nu\\|_Q\\leq\\|\\theta_*\\nu\\|_F\n                     \\leq C_\\theta\\|\\nu\\|_Q\n \\qquad(\\nu\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(Q)).\n \\label{ann:induction-norm}$$ Postcomposing $\\theta$ with an inner automorphism of $F$ does not change $\\theta_*$. Induction is functorial: if $\\psi\\colon F\\to F'$ is another injection of nontrivial finite-rank free groups, then $$(\\psi\\circ\\theta)_*=\\psi_*\\circ\\theta_*.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }

      "output": "Continuous functorial current induction with positive norm comparisons.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ann-current-induction"
    },

For every fixed nonempty reduced word $u$ in $F$ there is a constant $D_{\\theta,u}$, independent of $W$, such that $$\\left|\\eta_{\\theta(Wb)}\n       \\bigl(\\operatorname{Cyl}_F([1,u])\\bigr)\n       -N_u^{\\mathrm{sym}}\\bigl(\\operatorname{red}(\\theta(W))\\bigr)\n \\right|\\leq D_{\\theta,u}.\n \\label{ann:capping-frequency}$$ There is also a constant $D_\\theta$ independent of $W$ such that $$\\bigl|\\|\\eta_{\\theta(Wb)}\\|_F-|\\theta(W)|_F\\bigr|\n \\leq D_\\theta.\n \\label{ann:capping-length}$$ Here $\\operatorname{red}$ denotes the unique reduced representative. The same cap $b$ may be used simultaneously for any finite collection of fixed injections out of $Q$.",

More precisely, for $$K_s=f_{\\epsilon_s}(P)\\cap\n             a_s l_{\\epsilon_{s+1}}(P)a_s^{-1}$$ there is $\\nu_s\\in\\operatorname{Curr}(K_s)$ satisfying $$(\\theta_{s,0})_*\\nu_s=\\mu_s,\n \\qquad(\\theta_{s,1})_*\\nu_s=\\mu_{s+1},\n \\label{ann:finite-compatibility}$$ where $$\\theta_{s,0}=f_{\\epsilon_s}^{-1},\\qquad\n \\theta_{s,1}=l_{\\epsilon_{s+1}}^{-1}\n                \\circ(c\\mapsto a_s^{-1}ca_s).$$ All the groups $K_s$ are nontrivial finitely generated free groups.",

Let $L$ be an oriented line of $\\mathcal C_Q$, and let $L_a,L_b$ be its tightened image lines in $\\mathcal C_F,\\mathcal C_{F'}$. If $q\\in Q$ satisfies $d(a(q),L_a)\\le D$, then $$d(b(q),L_b)\\le E.$$ If two such elements $q_1,q_2$ have projections to $L_a$ positively ordered and separated by more than $T$, then their projections to $L_b$ are in the same order. Suppose a sign $\\epsilon\\in\\{+1,-1\\}$ satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x,\\beta^\\epsilon y)\\ge\\lambda l.$$ If every line of $\\mathcal A$ belongs to $\\partial^2\\Lambda S_\\epsilon$, then its image $\\mathcal A'=\\Theta_\\epsilon(\\mathcal A)$ has the same measure, lies in $\\mathcal U$, and admits vertices $x',y'$ on the tightened line $\\beta^\\epsilon L$ such that $$d_P(x',\\ell),d_P(y',\\ell)\\le Q\\quad(\\ell\\in\\mathcal A'),\n \\qquad d_M(x',y')\\ge2l.$$ The same sign satisfies $$d_M(\\beta^\\epsilon x',\\beta^\\epsilon y')\n \\ge\\lambda d_M(x',y').$$ The constants are independent of $L,x,y,\\mathcal A$ and of the number of times this assertion is applied.",

          "anchor": "stmt-ann-combination",
          "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a torsion-free word-hyperbolic group, let $A,B\\leq H$ be finitely generated free quasiconvex subgroups, and let $\\phi\\colon A\\to B$ be an isomorphism. Set $$G=\\langle H,t\\mid tat^{-1}=\\phi(a)\\ (a\\in A)\\rangle,\n \\qquad P=A,\\qquad I=A\\cap B,\\qquad J=t^{-1}It\\leq P.$$ Assume the following two conditions.*\n\n1.  *For $M,N\\in\\{A,B\\}$ and $g\\in H$, the intersection $M\\cap gNg^{-1}$ is at most cyclic unless $g\\in MN$.*\n\n\n2.  *The groups $I,J$ are finitely generated, and each $E\\in\\{I,J\\}$ satisfies $$\\sum_{SpE\\in S\\backslash P/E}\n            \\bar r(E\\cap p^{-1}Sp)\\leq\\bar r(S)\n          \\qquad\\text{for every finitely generated }S\\leq P.\n          \\label{ann:inertia-assumption}$$*\n\n*If $G$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$, then $G$ is word-hyperbolic.",

There are a finite-index subgroup $H\\leq G$, an integer $d\\geq0$, an automorphism $\\alpha$ of the rank-$d$ free group $F_d$, and an embedding $$H\\hookrightarrow F_d\\rtimes_\\alpha\\mathbb Z.$$ Consequently the same $H$ fits into an exact sequence $$1\\longrightarrow K\\longrightarrow H\\longrightarrow Q\\longrightarrow1,\n\\qquad K\\text{ free},\\quad Q\\leq\\mathbb Z.$$ Thus $G$ is virtually free-by-cyclic, with the rank of $K$ allowed to be infinite and $Q$ allowed to be trivial.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-one-relator-hyperbolicity**: A finitely generated one-relator group satisfying the stated Baumslag–Solitar exclusion is word-hyperbolic. The finite label checker below does not decide that exclusion.
- **invariant-zero-carrier-mass**: Each maximal carrier and each translated carrier line set has zero stationary-current mass. This measure conclusion alone does not exclude membership in the closed support.
- **invariant-periodic-label-agreement**: Periodic label sequences agreeing on the stated overlap agree everywhere. For oriented Cayley-tree lines, the shared actual segment also identifies their position.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-general-hnn-combination**: Apply the hyperbolicity conclusion to arbitrary HNN extensions over free quasiconvex subgroups. -> The required intersection restrictions and strong inertia have not been established.
- **anti-zero-mass-support-exclusion**: Remove every zero-mass carrier line from the closed support immediately. -> A closed set of zero measure can still contain support lines.
- **anti-unbounded-window-union**: Treat the fixed-window orbit bound as a bound for all window lengths together. -> The estimate fixes the window shape before counting its orbits.
- **anti-labels-without-position**: Identify two actual Cayley-tree lines solely because their repeating label patterns agree. -> Matching labels without a shared actual segment do not supply a common position in the tree.
- **anti-finite-check-global-certificate**: Treat passing periodic-word vectors as a certificate of the full hyperbolicity theorem. -> Finite periodic-label comparison does not check subgroup exclusion, stationary currents, or the Magnus induction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The periodic-label lemma admits a finite exact construction: nonempty finite label words specify their bi-infinite periodic sequences, and agreement over the sum of their lengths decides global label equality. This implementation covers that lemma only; the current compactness and hyperbolicity arguments are not certified by these finite tests.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON object with nonempty strings left and right; each character is a label.
# PRECONDITIONS: The strings specify periodic sequences with a common index origin and orientation.
# OUTPUTS: JSON booleans agree and equal for the prescribed overlap and global sequence equality.
# TIME: Linear in the sum of the two supplied period lengths.
# SPACE: Linear in the input size; comparison uses constant auxiliary space.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer indices and exact character equality.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and produce no accepted result.
import json
import sys

payload = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(payload, dict) or set(payload) != {'left', 'right'}:
    raise ValueError('expected left and right only')
left, right = payload['left'], payload['right']
if not isinstance(left, str) or not isinstance(right, str) or not left or not right:
    raise ValueError('period words must be nonempty strings')
a, b = len(left), len(right)
limit = a + b
agreed = all(left[i % a] == right[i % b] for i in range(limit))
equal = agreed
print(json.dumps({'agree': agreed, 'equal': equal}, sort_keys=True))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The proper-power branch supplies a finite word-reduction construction suitable for deciding whether an input word represents the identity in that presentation. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-one-relator-scope** (formal_scope): The main hyperbolicity conclusion requires a finite generating set and a one-relator presentation. Blocked use: ['Infer hyperbolicity for arbitrary finitely presented groups from Baumslag–Solitar exclusion alone.']
- **limit-full-subgroup-exclusion** (hypothesis): The exclusion concerns every Baumslag–Solitar subgroup with nonzero signed parameters, including the abelian obstruction. Blocked use: ['Replace subgroup exclusion by the absence of an obvious Baumslag–Solitar defining presentation.', 'Check only positive parameters without establishing the equivalent full exclusion.']
- **limit-magnus-subgraph-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The graph subgroup conclusion requires a nontrivial immersed non-power circuit, ambient Baumslag–Solitar exclusion, and a connected subgraph omitting part of the circuit. Blocked use: ['Apply Magnus quasiconvexity to a subgraph containing the entire attaching circuit.', 'Transfer the graph theorem directly to proper-power attaching circuits.']
- **limit-restricted-combination-data** (hypothesis): The combination theorem requires a torsion-free hyperbolic vertex group, finitely generated free quasiconvex edge groups, the specified conjugate-intersection restriction, and separate strong-inertia inequalities for both overlap groups. Blocked use: ['Apply the combination conclusion to arbitrary HNN extensions over free subgroups.', 'Establish inertia for only one overlap group and assume it for the other.']
- **limit-torsion-double-coset-qualification** (edge_case): Double-coset finiteness concerns intersections containing infinite-order elements; replacing that condition by nontriviality requires torsion-freeness. Blocked use: ['Enumerate all nontrivial conjugate intersections using this finiteness claim in an ambient group with torsion.']
- **limit-fixed-window-shape** (formal_scope): The window rank and orbit bounds apply separately to a fixed shape, rather than to the union over all lengths. Blocked use: ['Use the fixed-shape orbit bound as a finite enumeration bound for every window length together.']
- **limit-flaring-quantifier-order** (hypothesis): The expansion factor and row count must be fixed before varying the width bound; the girth threshold may depend on that width bound. Blocked use: ['Choose a new expansion factor or row count separately for each width and claim the stated flaring criterion.']
- **limit-counterannuli-local-uniformity** (formal_scope): Counterannulus bridges and intermediate ratio bounds are fixed within a chosen window length and may depend on that length. Blocked use: ['Assume a bridge bound uniform over growing windows before replacing bridges by finite transition types.', 'Interchange the girth limit and the growing-window limit without controlling the resulting errors.']
- **limit-based-versus-cyclic-length** (approximation): Annular girth uses based length, whereas counting-current norm uses cyclic length. The capping estimates require fixed injections, fixed bases, and a fixed test word. Blocked use: ['Normalize uncapped counting currents by based girth and assume that central mass survives.', 'Treat the cylinder discrepancy constant as independent of the injection or test word.']
- **limit-current-induction-not-linear-injectivity** (formal_scope): An injective group homomorphism induces a nonzero-preserving current map with norm comparisons; injectivity of that linear map is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Recover a unique source current from its induced target current.', 'Replace coset induction by an ordinary boundary push and assume target-group invariance.']
- **limit-current-compactness-not-finite-extraction** (nonconstructive): The bilateral trajectory is obtained through compactness and diagonal subsequence extraction, rather than a finite procedure producing all its currents. Blocked use: ['Treat a finite sample of counterannuli as a certificate of the entire bilateral trajectory.', 'Claim an executable global hyperbolicity test from the current-limit construction.']
- **limit-stationary-limit-atomlessness** (nonconstructive): Stationarity uses normalized averaging or a positive bilateral sum. Atomlessness must be proved again after taking the stationary limit. Blocked use: ['Assume a weak limit is atomless merely because every approximating current is atomless.', 'Use normalized averaging when total transition mass remains bounded without handling the summable case.']
- **limit-conull-versus-entire-support** (formal_scope): Initially, iterable transfers are established on an invariant conull Borel set. Extension to every support line requires the later periodic-support exclusion and continuity argument. Blocked use: ['Transfer an arbitrary support line using only the initial conull construction.', 'Assume density of the conull set makes it closed.']
- **limit-natural-graph-versus-label-length** (formal_scope): Suppressing degree-two interiors bounds natural graph size but does not bound subdivided edge lengths. Blocked use: ['Size storage for all window cores from the natural-edge bound alone.', 'Assume bounded rank makes all edge labels uniformly short.']
- **limit-small-graph-subsequence** (nonconstructive): The small graph depends on subsequence choices fixing abstract graph type, bounded labels, and prefix limits of divergent labels. Blocked use: ['Read a canonical small graph directly from an arbitrary finite window core.', 'Assume a finite initial sequence identifies every divergent natural edge.']
- **limit-carriers-may-be-absent** (edge_case): The small graph may have no noncyclic components. Carrier-return assertions then provide no maximal carrier to instantiate. Blocked use: ['Require a maximal noncyclic carrier to exist solely from the finite-cofinality proposition.']
- **limit-zero-mass-not-support-exclusion** (formal_scope): Zero mass on a maximal carrier does not by itself exclude carrier lines from closed support. Blocked use: ['Delete carrier support lines immediately after proving zero carrier mass.', 'Skip the finite-failure and periodic-support arguments.']
- **limit-fixed-transfer-and-trimming** (hypothesis): Transport constants depend on fixed embeddings and bases. Repeated trimming requires a sufficiently long segment, an expanding direction, and membership of every transported line in the prescribed return domain. Blocked use: ['Apply the same constants across unspecified changing embeddings.', 'Continue a return after a line leaves its domain.', 'Apply the growth conclusion to segments below the fixed threshold.']
- **limit-failure-axis-support-status** (formal_scope): Finite failure supplies a support line and a periodic axis sharing a long segment. The periodic axis itself is not asserted to belong to the support at that stage. Blocked use: ['Treat a single failure axis as a periodic support witness without the recentering and closedness argument.']
- **limit-whole-period-pullback** (formal_scope): Pullback controls whole translation periods rather than metric length. Primitive translations must be interpreted in the specified group and need not be basis elements. Blocked use: ['Pull back only an overlap-length estimate while ignoring growth of primitive periods.', 'Identify the primitive ambient translation with the primitive carrier translation automatically.']
- **limit-periodic-label-geometric-position** (hypothesis): Periodic-pattern agreement identifies label sequences. Identifying actual Cayley-tree lines additionally requires the stated shared actual segment and orientation. Blocked use: ['Identify translated axes solely from identical repeating label patterns.', 'Treat short prefix agreement as global equality without the required overlap.']
- **limit-magnus-induction-branching** (hypothesis): The decreasing Magnus splitting requires the stated non-power and graph-rank hypotheses. Trivial relators and proper powers are handled by separate reductions. Blocked use: ['Apply the non-power splitting unchanged to a proper-power relator.', 'Omit verification of strict complexity decrease or injection of the vertex group.']
- **limit-dehn-construction-proper-power-branch** (formal_scope): The explicit shortening construction is justified by the proper-power spelling theorem. The non-power hyperbolicity proof does not supply those same shortening rules for the original presentation. Blocked use: ['Run the proper-power relator-shortening procedure on an arbitrary non-power presentation and claim completeness.']
- **limit-virtual-embedding-external-dependence** (formal_scope): The virtual free-by-cyclic consequence also uses companion virtual compact specialness and external fibering and embedding results. Blocked use: ['Attribute the virtual embedding conclusion to the hyperbolicity theorem alone.', 'Claim an explicit extraction procedure for embedding data from the existential corollary.']
- **limit-restricted-kernel-rank** (edge_case): The restricted free kernel may have infinite rank and the cyclic image may be trivial, despite finite generation of the ambient free kernel. Blocked use: ['Require finitely many generators for the restricted kernel without an additional hypothesis.', 'Identify the restricted kernel with a canonical height kernel in the ascending-HNN consequence.']

There are constants $L_D,B_D$ such that if a periodic $P$-line $\\zeta$ of primitive period at most $D$ shares a segment of length at least $L_D$ with an $M$-line, then $\\zeta$ is itself an $M$-line. Its primitive translation in $M$ has cyclic $M$-length at most $B_D$.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],

        }

          "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\operatorname{rank}\\pi_1\\Gamma\\geq2$ and that $\\lambda$ represents a non-power in $\\pi_1\\Gamma$. There are a domain $U\\subset\\widetilde\\Gamma$ and a closed lift $\\widetilde\\lambda\\colon S^1\\to U$ with the following properties. Set $$U_-=U\\cap\\tau^{-1}U,\\qquad U_+=U\\cap\\tau U,\n \\qquad Z=U\\cup_{\\widetilde\\lambda}D^2.$$ Both $U_-$ and $U_+$ are connected nonempty Magnus subgraphs of $Z$. Either both are trees, or $U_-\\cap U_+$ is connected and nonempty. Their fundamental groups are simultaneous Magnus subgroups in a presentation of $H=\\pi_1Z$, and $$\\label{mag:hnn}\n \\pi_1X\\cong\\langle H,t\\mid tAt^{-1}=B\\rangle,\n \\qquad A=\\pi_1U_-,\\quad B=\\pi_1U_+,$$ with edge isomorphism induced by $\\tau$ and suitable basepoint paths. The vertex and edge groups inject, the lifted relator is a non-power, and $$c(U,\\widetilde\\lambda)<c(\\Gamma,\\lambda).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity-2",
          "exact_quote": "If it becomes trivial, the group is a finitely generated free group and is hyperbolic.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-magnus-induction-branching",
      "kind": "hypothesis",

      "support": "inferred"
    },
    {

        "Run the proper-power relator-shortening procedure on an arbitrary non-power presentation and claim completeness."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-mag-nonpower-hyperbolicity-2",
          "exact_quote": "Replacing that segment by the inverse complementary segment strictly shortens the word and uses one defining relation. Repeating, with free reduction after each replacement, fills a null word with at most its original length in relator cells.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-dehn-construction-proper-power-branch",
      "kind": "formal_scope",

      "support": "inferred"

        "Claim an explicit extraction procedure for embedding data from the existential corollary."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:free-by-cyclic",
          "exact_quote": "We now combine the hyperbolicity theorem with the companion virtual compact specialness theorem and the results of Kielak–Linton.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-free-by-cyclic",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be finite, let $r\\in F(X)$, and suppose that $G=F(X)/\\langle\\!\\langle r\\rangle\\!\\rangle$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $\\operatorname{BS}(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$. There are a finite-index subgroup $H\\leq G$, an integer $d\\geq0$, an automorphism $\\alpha$ of the rank-$d$ free group $F_d$, and an embedding $$H\\hookrightarrow F_d\\rtimes_\\alpha\\mathbb Z.$$ Consequently the same $H$ fits into an exact sequence $$1\\longrightarrow K\\longrightarrow H\\longrightarrow Q\\longrightarrow1,\n\\qquad K\\text{ free},\\quad Q\\leq\\mathbb Z.$$ Thus $G$ is virtually free-by-cyclic, with the rank of $K$ allowed to be infinite and $Q$ allowed to be trivial.",
          "source_id": "PM-258.1"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Gersten's conjecture: every finitely generated one-relator group containing no subgroup isomorphic to a Baumslag–Solitar group $`\mathop{\mathrm{BS}}\nolimits (m,n)`$, for nonzero integers <i>m</i>, <i>n</i>, is word-hyperbolic.

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

A single defining relation can produce very different geometries. The Baumslag–Solitar groups $$\operatorname{BS}(m,n)
   =\langle a,t\mid ta^mt^{-1}=a^n\rangle,
 \qquad m,n\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\},$$ are the basic obstructions to hyperbolicity among one-relator groups. Gersten asked whether they are the only subgroup obstructions (Gersten 1992, 228) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Gersten1992Problems](#ref-Gersten1992Problems)]. We prove the following statement, resolving Gersten’s conjecture positively. Here $F(X)$ denotes the free group on $X$, and $\langle X\mid r\rangle$ is its quotient by the normal closure of $r$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a finite set and let $r\in F(X)$. If the one-relator group $G=\langle X\mid r\rangle$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $\operatorname{BS}(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$, then $G$ is word-hyperbolic.*

</div>

The parameters in this statement may have either sign. In particular, the hypothesis excludes $\operatorname{BS}(1,1)=\mathbb Z^2$. We call a group with this exclusion *Baumslag–Solitar-free*. The customary formulation excluding every $\operatorname{BS}(1,n)$, for every nonzero integer $n$, is equivalent: every Baumslag–Solitar group contains one of these groups (Gardam, Kielak, and Logan 2025, Lemma 2.10) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[GardamKielakLogan2025Algebraically](#ref-GardamKielakLogan2025Algebraically)]. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> includes trivial relators and proper powers; it has no torsion-free hypothesis.

We use *free-by-cyclic* in the subgroup-closed sense: a group $H$ fits into an exact sequence $$1\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow H\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow1$$ with $K$ free of possibly infinite rank and $Q\leq\mathbb Z$, possibly trivial. Kielak–Linton recall Wise’s Conjecture 17.8 that every hyperbolic one-relator group is virtually free-by-cyclic and prove the conclusion under the additional hypothesis of virtual compact specialness (Kielak and Linton 2024, sec. 1.1 and Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KielakLinton2024Virtually](#ref-KielakLinton2024Virtually)]. The companion theorem (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[OpenAIVirtualSpecialness](#ref-OpenAIVirtualSpecialness)] provides that hypothesis. Combining it with Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives the Baumslag–Solitar-free form in Corollary <a href="#cor:free-by-cyclic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:free-by-cyclic">55</a>, together with a finite-index embedding into a free-by-$\mathbb Z$ group with finitely generated free kernel.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Earlier work and the remaining step

The classical theory already separates the torsion case from the main difficulty. Magnus’s Freiheitssatz identifies free subgroups generated by suitable proper subsets of a one-relator generating set (Magnus 1930) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Magnus1930](#ref-Magnus1930)]. Newman’s spelling theorem for proper-power relators gives a Dehn algorithm and hence hyperbolicity (Newman 1968, Theorem 3) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Newman1968](#ref-Newman1968)]; see also the proof in (McCool and Schupp 1973, Theorem 4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[McCoolSchupp1973](#ref-McCoolSchupp1973)]. Thus the new hyperbolicity assertion concerns non-power relators, although Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> includes all relators. The Freiheitssatz makes an inductive approach possible: a Magnus splitting has a smaller one-relator vertex group and free edge groups. The geometric form of the Magnus–Moldavanskii–Masters hierarchy, developed by Linton, realizes these splittings through domains in cyclic covers (Linton 2025a, secs. 1.1–1.2 and 4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LintonHierarchies2025](#ref-LintonHierarchies2025)]. We use that construction in Section <a href="#mag:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mag:section">8</a>, with the domain and complexity arguments given in the form needed here.

Several recent results identify substantial classes where this induction preserves hyperbolicity. Louder and Wilton characterized negative immersions for one-relator presentation complexes by primitivity rank greater than two (Louder and Wilton 2022, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[LouderWilton2022NegativeImmersions](#ref-LouderWilton2022NegativeImmersions)]. Here the primitivity rank of a relator $r$ is the least rank of a subgroup of $F(X)$ containing $r$ in which $r$ is not part of a free basis, with value $\infty$ if no such subgroup exists. Their subgroup theorem excludes Baumslag–Solitar subgroups in this class, and their subsequent work establishes uniform negative immersions and coherence (Louder and Wilton 2022, Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[LouderWilton2022NegativeImmersions](#ref-LouderWilton2022NegativeImmersions)] <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[LouderWilton2024UniformNegativeImmersions](#ref-LouderWilton2024UniformNegativeImmersions)] (Louder and Wilton 2024, Theorems A and C). Linton then proved hyperbolicity and virtual specialness for groups with negative immersions (Linton 2025a, Theorem 7.2) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[LintonHierarchies2025](#ref-LintonHierarchies2025)]. His later characterization says that a finitely generated one-relator group is both hyperbolic and locally quasiconvex precisely when its relator has primitivity rank different from two (Linton 2025b, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Linton2025MappingToriSubgroups](#ref-Linton2025MappingToriSubgroups)]. Local quasiconvexity means that every finitely generated subgroup is quasiconvex. This characterization does not settle hyperbolicity alone for Baumslag–Solitar-free groups whose relator has primitivity rank two.

Linton’s hierarchy criterion makes the remaining obstruction more specific: a one-relator hierarchy is quasiconvex with hyperbolic ambient group if and only if it is $\mathbb Z$-stable and the group is Baumslag–Solitar-free (Linton 2025a, Theorem 7.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[LintonHierarchies2025](#ref-LintonHierarchies2025)]. The stability hypothesis controls the persistence of noncyclic intersections along the hierarchy; it is an additional condition, not a consequence supplied by that criterion. A complementary reduction to primitive-extension groups isolates a particular class of two-generator one-relator groups in which to settle the conjecture (Linton 2026, Theorem 5.6 and Corollary 5.7) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LintonHyperbolic2026](#ref-LintonHyperbolic2026)]. For a broader historical account, see (Linton and Nyberg-Brodda 2025) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LintonNybergBrodda2025Survey](#ref-LintonNybergBrodda2025Survey)].

The geometric issue is expansion in a graph of groups. The Bestvina–Feighn combination theorem makes annular flaring a sufficient condition for hyperbolicity when the vertex and edge data satisfy the required geometric hypotheses (Bestvina and Feighn 1996, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BestvinaFeighn1996Addendum](#ref-BestvinaFeighn1996Addendum)]. We use the algebraic annular formulation in (Kapovich 1999, Theorem 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Kapovich1999NonQuasiconvexity](#ref-Kapovich1999NonQuasiconvexity)]. For mapping tori of free-group automorphisms, Brinkmann proved that the absence of periodic conjugacy classes forces expansion and hyperbolicity (Brinkmann 2000) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Brinkmann2000HyperbolicAutomorphisms](#ref-Brinkmann2000HyperbolicAutomorphisms)]. Mutanguha extended the hyperbolicity criterion to ascending HNN extensions of finite-rank free groups: they are hyperbolic exactly when they contain no Baumslag–Solitar subgroup (Mutanguha 2021, Theorem 6.7) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Mutanguha2021](#ref-Mutanguha2021)]. His expanding relative immersions, iterated pullbacks, and annular arguments supply important precedents for treating persistent intersections (Mutanguha 2021, Theorem 4.5 and Sections 5–6) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Mutanguha2021](#ref-Mutanguha2021)]. The combination theorem here permits both free edge subgroups to be proper in a hyperbolic vertex group, under the intersection and rank hypotheses below.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The combination argument

Our main technical result is a combination theorem for a restricted class of HNN extensions of torsion-free hyperbolic groups (Theorem <a href="#ann:combination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:combination">10</a>). The Magnus construction reduces the problem to a one-relator vertex group of smaller complexity and finitely generated free edge groups. Once the vertex group is hyperbolic, Linton’s quasiconvexity theorem gives quasiconvex edge groups. Two further intersection properties are needed: conjugates of the edge groups have noncyclic intersection only in one specified double coset, and their overlap subgroups satisfy a strong reduced-rank inequality. Collins’s intersection theorem and Linton’s strong-inertia result supply these properties for the Magnus splittings used here (Collins 2008; Linton 2025a) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Collins2008Conjugates](#ref-Collins2008Conjugates), [LintonHierarchies2025](#ref-LintonHierarchies2025)]. The combination theorem shows that the absence of Baumslag–Solitar subgroups forces annular flaring under precisely these hypotheses.

We prove flaring by contradiction, using free-group currents: invariant measures on pairs of distinct boundary points (Kapovich 2006) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[KapovichCurrents2006](#ref-KapovichCurrents2006)]. Long nonflaring annuli give a nonzero stationary current for which almost every line can be transferred across successive edges of the Bass–Serre tree. The stabilizers of increasingly long tree paths are represented by finite labeled core graphs. The rank inequality bounds the number of edges after degree-two vertices are suppressed; it does not bound their label lengths. Along a subsequence, the edges with bounded labels form one fixed graph, called the small subgraph. Its noncyclic components determine finitely many maximal subgroup carriers for persistent path stabilizers. A return along a carrier induces an atoroidal automorphism, with no nontrivial periodic conjugacy class. Brinkmann’s expansion theorem then forces the set of carrier lines to have zero current mass.

Zero mass does not exclude a carrier line from the closed support of the current. This distinction is the main issue in Section <a href="#exp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="exp:section">6</a>. Starting with a putative carrier line in the support, we transport a positive-mass cylinder along expanding carrier returns. If this transport continues to follow the prescribed carrier, recentering and compactness concentrate positive mass on its zero-mass line set. If it fails, the stabilizers of the competing actual tree path and the prescribed path have at most cyclic intersection. A sufficiently long overlap consequently follows a periodic axis of uniformly bounded primitive period. Recentering these overlaps puts a periodic line in the support. We reuse this same failure mechanism to exclude periodic support lines: pulling the overlap back along the carrier return preserves enough whole periods to identify the failure axis with the original periodic trajectory, contradicting the competing path choices. Counting whole periods is essential because inverse automorphisms can enlarge primitive period lengths.

It follows that no support line lies in the small subgraph and that every support line has irrational endpoints. The bounded graph complexity now leaves only finitely many rays admitting two different pasts. Transfer permutes their finitely many endpoint orbits under the free edge group. A return at one of these endpoints supplies infinitely many disjoint translates of a fixed positive-mass set inside a compact set, contradicting local finiteness of the current. This proves the restricted combination theorem; the geometric Magnus induction then proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Magnus subgraphs

We also record the geometric subgroup conclusion in the scope needed by the induction. A *circuit* in a graph is a map from a subdivided circle; it is *immersed* when its edge path has no backtracking, including at the closing point. A nontrivial element is a *non-power* if it is not $u^k$ with $|k|\geq2$.

<a id="stmt-thm-magnus"></a>
<div id="thm:magnus" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *Let $\Gamma$ be a finite connected graph, and let $\lambda:S^1\to\Gamma$ be a nontrivial immersed circuit representing a non-power in $\pi_1\Gamma$. Attach a $2$-cell along $\lambda$, obtaining $Y$. If $\pi_1Y$ is Baumslag–Solitar-free, then, for every connected subgraph $S\subseteq\Gamma$ with $\lambda(S^1)\nsubseteq S$, the map $$\pi_1S\longrightarrow\pi_1Y$$ is injective and its image is quasiconvex.*

</div>

A choice of basepoint paths changes these subgroups only by conjugacy. Such an $S$ is called a *Magnus subgraph*. Its injectivity follows from the Freiheitssatz. Once Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives ambient hyperbolicity, its quasiconvexity follows from Linton’s theorem that Magnus subgroups of hyperbolic one-relator groups are quasiconvex (Linton 2026, Theorem 4.7) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[LintonHyperbolic2026](#ref-LintonHyperbolic2026)]. We prove the graph-to-basis identification explicitly in Lemma <a href="#mag:graph-magnus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mag:graph-magnus">51</a>. The same quasiconvexity theorem supplies the quasiconvex edge groups at each inductive step, so the induction needs only to establish hyperbolicity of the smaller vertex group.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Structure of the proof

Sections <a href="#pre:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:section">2</a>–<a href="#ray:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ray:section">7</a> prove the restricted combination theorem. Section <a href="#ann:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:section">3</a> states its hypotheses and converts nonflaring annuli into a bounded bilateral sequence of currents. Section <a href="#stat:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="stat:section">4</a> removes atoms and obtains a stationary current with well-defined transfers on a conull set. Sections <a href="#car:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="car:section">5</a> and <a href="#exp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="exp:section">6</a> construct the finite carriers and exclude periodic support lines. Section <a href="#ray:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ray:section">7</a> finishes the measure contradiction. Finally, Section <a href="#mag:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mag:section">8</a> constructs a decreasing Magnus induction and proves Theorems <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and <a href="#thm:magnus" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:magnus">2</a>. Section <a href="#sec:free-by-cyclic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:free-by-cyclic">9</a> derives the virtual free-by-cyclic consequence and records the corresponding ascending-HNN consequence of the companion theorem.

<a id="pre:section"></a>
### Intersections, cyclic commensurators, and rank bounds

We need three preliminary tools. The first controls intersections of quasiconvex subgroups. The second produces an embedded Baumslag–Solitar subgroup from a tree action. The third bounds the total reduced rank of a family of conjugate intersections.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Quasiconvex intersections

All word metrics come from finite generating sets. A group is *word-hyperbolic* if geodesic triangles in a Cayley graph are uniformly thin: each side lies in a fixed neighborhood of the other two. A subgroup of a hyperbolic group is *quasiconvex* if every geodesic between its elements lies in a fixed neighborhood of the subgroup. We use the Morse lemma and its usual consequences: quasiconvex subgroups are finitely generated and quasi-isometrically embedded, these two properties characterize quasiconvexity, and infinite cyclic subgroups are quasiconvex. In particular, quasiconvexity is transitive between hyperbolic groups, since quasi-isometric embeddings compose. For these background facts, see (Bridson and Haefliger 1999, Theorem III.H.1.7, Corollary III.$\Gamma$.3.6(2), and Corollary III.$\Gamma$.3.10(1)) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[BridsonHaefliger1999](#ref-BridsonHaefliger1999)]. For a subset $Q$ of a hyperbolic group, write $\Lambda Q$ for its accumulation set in the boundary.

<a id="stmt-pre-quasiconvex-intersection"></a>
<div id="pre:quasiconvex-intersection" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Quasiconvex intersections). *Let $K,L$ be quasiconvex subgroups of a finitely generated hyperbolic group $H$. Then $K\cap L$ is quasiconvex and $$\Lambda(K\cap L)=\Lambda K\cap\Lambda L.$$ The same assertions hold for any finite family of quasiconvex subgroups.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-quasiconvex-intersection"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-quasiconvex-intersection</summary>

<a id="pre:neighborhood-intersection"></a>
*Proof.* First observe that, for fixed subgroups $K,L$ of any finitely generated group and $R\geq 0$, there is a constant $D$ such that $$N_R(K)\cap N_R(L)\subseteq N_D(K\cap L).
  \label{pre:neighborhood-intersection}$$ Indeed, if $x$ belongs to the left side, choose $k\in K$ and $l\in L$ within distance $R$ of $x$. The element $d=k^{-1}l$ lies in the finite ball of radius $2R$. For each value $d$ that occurs, fix one pair $k_d\in K,l_d\in L$ with $k_d^{-1}l_d=d$. Then $$z=kk_d^{-1}=ll_d^{-1}\in K\cap L,
  \qquad d(x,z)\leq R+|k_d|.$$ Taking a maximum over the finitely many $d$ proves <a href="#pre:neighborhood-intersection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:neighborhood-intersection">[pre:neighborhood-intersection]</a>.

A geodesic with endpoints in $K\cap L$ lies in fixed neighborhoods of both $K$ and $L$, so <a href="#pre:neighborhood-intersection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:neighborhood-intersection">[pre:neighborhood-intersection]</a> proves quasiconvexity of their intersection.

Now let $\xi\in\Lambda K\cap\Lambda L$. Quasiconvexity and limits of geodesic segments show that a ray from the identity to $\xi$ lies in bounded neighborhoods of both subgroups. Applying <a href="#pre:neighborhood-intersection" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:neighborhood-intersection">[pre:neighborhood-intersection]</a> along the ray gives a sequence in $K\cap L$ converging to $\xi$. Thus $\xi\in\Lambda(K\cap L)$. The reverse inclusion is immediate, and induction gives the finite-family assertions. These facts also appear in (Gitik et al. 1998, Lemmas 2.6 and 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[GitikMitraRipsSageev1998](#ref-GitikMitraRipsSageev1998)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pre-double-coset-finiteness"></a>
<div id="pre:double-coset-finiteness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Finite double-coset lists). *Let $K,L$ be quasiconvex subgroups of a finitely generated hyperbolic group $H$. There are only finitely many double cosets $KgL$ such that $K\cap gLg^{-1}$ contains an infinite-order element. If $H$ is torsion-free, there are therefore only finitely many such double cosets with nontrivial intersection.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-double-coset-finiteness"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-double-coset-finiteness</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose that $c\in K\cap gLg^{-1}$ has infinite order. Its two fixed boundary points belong to both $\Lambda K$ and $\Lambda(gL)$: use the sequences $c^n\in K$ and $c^ng\in gL$, for positive and negative $n$. A geodesic line between those points lies in a uniformly bounded neighborhood of each of $K$ and $gL$. To see the uniformity, a $q$-quasiconvex set contains finite geodesics between its points within distance $q$; passing to limits, and then comparing geodesics with the same ideal endpoints, increases this bound by a constant depending only on the hyperbolicity constant. The quasiconvexity constant of $gL$ equals that of $L$.

At any point of this line choose nearby vertices $k\in K$ and $gl\in gL$. Their distance is bounded by a constant depending only on the two quasiconvexity constants and the hyperbolicity constant. Hence $k^{-1}gl\in KgL$ lies in a fixed finite ball. Every double coset under consideration meets that ball, proving finiteness. ◻

</details>

The qualification about infinite-order elements is necessary in groups with torsion. For example, the central subgroup $C_2$ of the hyperbolic group $F_2\times C_2$ has nontrivial intersection with every conjugate, but there are infinitely many $C_2$-double cosets.

For later applications to cosets, note also that $$\Lambda(gL)=\Lambda(gLg^{-1}).$$ Indeed, right multiplication by the fixed element $g^{-1}$ moves each point of $gL$ a bounded distance.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Commensuration in a tree action

Two subgroups are *commensurable* if their intersection has finite index in both. For a subgroup $C\leq H$, its commensurator is $$\operatorname{Comm}_H(C)
  =\{h\in H: C\text{ and }hCh^{-1}\text{ are commensurable}\}.$$ This is a subgroup. We first deduce the cyclic case directly from the double-coset lemma.

<a id="stmt-pre-cyclic-commensurator"></a>
<div id="pre:cyclic-commensurator" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *If $C$ is an infinite cyclic subgroup of a hyperbolic group $H$, then $C$ has finite index in $\operatorname{Comm}_H(C)$. If $H$ is torsion-free, this commensurator is infinite cyclic.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-cyclic-commensurator"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-cyclic-commensurator</summary>

*Proof.* The subgroup $C$ is quasiconvex. By Lemma <a href="#pre:double-coset-finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:double-coset-finiteness">4</a>, its commensurator meets only finitely many double cosets $CgC$. For a representative $g$ in the commensurator, $CgC$ is a union of $[C:C\cap g^{-1}Cg]$ left $C$-cosets, a finite number. Thus the commensurator is virtually cyclic. A torsion-free virtually cyclic group is cyclic, giving the last assertion. ◻

</details>

All tree actions below are actions on simplicial trees without inversions. An element is *elliptic* if it fixes a vertex and *tree-hyperbolic* if it translates a bi-infinite geodesic. We will need an embedded Baumslag–Solitar group, rather than just a power-conjugacy relation. The following normal-form argument provides it.

<a id="stmt-pre-gbs-subgroup"></a>
<div id="pre:gbs-subgroup" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Graphs of cyclic groups). *Let $Q$ be a finitely generated noncyclic group acting on a simplicial tree without inversions. If every vertex and edge stabilizer is infinite cyclic, then $Q$ contains a subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for some nonzero integers $m,n$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-gbs-subgroup"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-gbs-subgroup</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a vertex $v$ and a finite symmetric generating set $S$ of $Q$. The union of all $Q$-translates of the finite subtree $$D=\bigcup_{s\in S}[v,sv]$$ is connected: its segments connect the vertices in the orbit $Qv$ according to the Cayley graph of $Q$. This union is exactly the convex hull of $Qv$, because its segments join orbit points. Restricting the action to this hull gives a finite quotient, since every edge orbit has a representative in $D$. The retained vertex and edge stabilizers are unchanged. Bass–Serre theory therefore realizes $Q$ as the fundamental group of a finite connected graph of infinite cyclic groups.

Collapse any non-loop edge whose map to one endpoint group is onto. Such a collapse preserves the fundamental group and leaves a graph of infinite cyclic groups. After finitely many collapses, every remaining non-loop edge has both inclusion indices greater than one.

If a loop remains, the subgraph consisting of that loop and its vertex has fundamental group $$\langle x,t\mid t x^m t^{-1}=x^n\rangle=BS(m,n),
  \qquad m,n\neq 0.$$ Its fundamental group injects into $Q$ by the graph-of-groups normal form theorem.

If a non-loop edge remains, its two-vertex subgraph likewise injects and has fundamental group $$\langle x,y\mid x^m=y^n\rangle,
  \qquad |m|,|n|>1.$$ The element $z=x^m=y^n$ is central and has infinite order. Modulo $\langle z\rangle$, this group is $C_{|m|}*C_{|n|}$, in which the image of $xy$ has infinite order by free-product normal form. Thus the commuting elements $z$ and $xy$ generate an embedded $\mathbb Z^2=BS(1,1)$.

Finally, if no edge remains, the graph has one vertex and $Q$ is cyclic, contrary to the hypothesis. This exhausts the possibilities. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-pre-elliptic-commensurator"></a>
<div id="pre:elliptic-commensurator" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Elliptic commensuration). *Suppose that $G$ acts on a simplicial tree $\mathcal T$ without inversions, with torsion-free hyperbolic vertex stabilizers. If an infinite-order elliptic element $a\in G$ has its cyclic subgroup commensurated by a tree-hyperbolic element $g\in G$, then $G$ contains a subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for some nonzero integers $m,n$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-elliptic-commensurator"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-elliptic-commensurator</summary>

*Proof.* Set $L=\langle a,g\rangle$ and choose a vertex $v$ fixed by $a$. Every element of $L$ commensurates $\langle a\rangle$. For each $h\in L$, commensurability supplies nonzero integers $r,s$ with $a^r=ha^sh^{-1}$; hence a nonzero power of $a$ fixes $hv$. A common multiple of finitely many such powers fixes any prescribed finite subset of $Lv$, and therefore fixes its convex hull pointwise. It follows that every finite subtree of $\mathcal T_L=\operatorname{Hull}(Lv)$ is fixed by a nonzero power of $a$.

Let $x$ be a vertex of $\mathcal T_L$, and choose $N>0$ with $a^N\in L_x$. Every element of $L_x$ commensurates $\langle a^N\rangle$, now a subgroup of the vertex stabilizer $G_x$. Thus $$\langle a^N\rangle
  \leq L_x
  \leq \operatorname{Comm}_{G_x}(\langle a^N\rangle).$$ Lemma <a href="#pre:cyclic-commensurator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:cyclic-commensurator">5</a> makes the last group infinite cyclic, so $L_x$ is infinite cyclic as well. Each edge stabilizer of $\mathcal T_L$ contains a nonzero power of $a$ and is a subgroup of a vertex stabilizer in $L$; it too is infinite cyclic.

The group $L$ is noncyclic. Otherwise, writing $a=c^r$ and $g=c^s$ in a cyclic group, translation lengths would give $\ell(a)=|r|\ell(c)=0$ and hence $\ell(g)=0$, contrary to tree-hyperbolicity of $g$. Apply Lemma <a href="#pre:gbs-subgroup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:gbs-subgroup">6</a> to the action of the finitely generated group $L$ on $\mathcal T_L$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Strong inertia in free groups

We now formulate the rank estimate that will control noncyclic intersections. For a finitely generated free group $K$, put $$\bar r(K)=\max\{\operatorname{rk}(K)-1,0\}.$$ Strong inertia bounds the sum of reduced ranks of all conjugate intersections; see (Linton 2025a) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[LintonHierarchies2025](#ref-LintonHierarchies2025)].

<a id="stmt-pre-strong-inertia-definition"></a>
<div id="pre:strong-inertia-definition" class="definition">

<a id="pre:strong-inertia-inequality"></a>
**Definition 8**. A finitely generated subgroup $E$ of a finitely generated free group $P$ is *strongly inert* if, for every finitely generated subgroup $S\leq P$, $$\sum_{SpE\in S\backslash P/E}
  \bar r(E\cap p^{-1}Sp)\leq\bar r(S).
  \label{pre:strong-inertia-inequality}$$ The summands are independent of the chosen double-coset representatives. Only finitely many intersections are nontrivial, by Lemma <a href="#pre:double-coset-finiteness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:double-coset-finiteness">4</a>.

</div>

<a id="stmt-pre-cyclic-off-diagonal"></a>
<div id="pre:cyclic-off-diagonal" class="corollary">

**Corollary 9**. *Let $P$ be a finitely generated free group and $E\leq P$ a strongly inert subgroup. If $g\in P\setminus E$, then $E\cap gEg^{-1}$ is trivial or cyclic.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-cyclic-off-diagonal"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-cyclic-off-diagonal</summary>

*Proof.* Apply <a href="#pre:strong-inertia-inequality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:strong-inertia-inequality">[pre:strong-inertia-inequality]</a> with $S=E$. The identity double coset contributes $\bar r(E)$, so all other nonnegative summands vanish. ◻

</details>

<a id="ann:section"></a>
### Annuli and currents for a restricted HNN extension

We now isolate the combination statement used in the Magnus induction. The restrictions on intersections in its hypotheses will provide a uniform bound on the ranks of path stabilizers. In this section we establish that failure of hyperbolicity produces a bounded, nonzero trajectory of free-group currents. The subsequent sections rule out that trajectory and thereby complete the combination argument.

<a id="stmt-ann-combination"></a>
<div id="ann:combination" class="theorem">

**Theorem 10** (Restricted combination theorem). *Let $H$ be a torsion-free word-hyperbolic group, let $A,B\leq H$ be finitely generated free quasiconvex subgroups, and let $\phi\colon A\to B$ be an isomorphism. Set $$G=\langle H,t\mid tat^{-1}=\phi(a)\ (a\in A)\rangle,
 \qquad P=A,\qquad I=A\cap B,\qquad J=t^{-1}It\leq P.$$ Assume the following two conditions.*

1.  *For $M,N\in\{A,B\}$ and $g\in H$, the intersection $M\cap gNg^{-1}$ is at most cyclic unless $g\in MN$.*

<a id="ann:inertia-assumption"></a>
2.  *The groups $I,J$ are finitely generated, and each $E\in\{I,J\}$ satisfies $$\sum_{SpE\in S\backslash P/E}
            \bar r(E\cap p^{-1}Sp)\leq\bar r(S)
          \qquad\text{for every finitely generated }S\leq P.
          \label{ann:inertia-assumption}$$*

*If $G$ contains no subgroup isomorphic to $BS(m,n)$ for any nonzero integers $m,n$, then $G$ is word-hyperbolic.*

</div>

All the intersection restrictions in this statement are hypotheses. In particular, the assertion concerns a restricted class of HNN extensions. We retain the notation of Theorem <a href="#ann:combination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:combination">10</a> throughout the combination argument. Write $$i\colon I\longrightarrow P,\quad i(c)=c,
 \qquad
 j\colon I\longrightarrow P,\quad j(c)=t^{-1}ct,$$ so that $j(I)=J$. Fix finite generating sets for $H$ and a free basis for $P$. We write $|\cdot|_H$ and $|\cdot|_P$ for the associated based word lengths. If $P=1$, then $G=H*\mathbb Z$ is hyperbolic, so we may assume $P\ne1$ in what follows.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Frames and stabilizers of paths

Let $\mathcal T$ be the Bass–Serre tree of the HNN extension. The height homomorphism $G\to\mathbb Z$ sends $t$ to $1$ and $H$ to $0$. Orient the canonical edge $e$ from $t^{-1}H$ to $H$, and orient every translate equivariantly. Its stabilizer is $P$. A *frame* for the edge $he$ is the element $h\in G$; changing $h$ to $hp$, with $p\in P$, changes the coordinates on its stabilizer but not the edge. A path traverses an edge positively if it follows this orientation.

<a id="stmt-ann-frames"></a>
<div id="ann:frames" class="lemma">

**Lemma 11** (Neighboring edges and frames). *A reduced path in $\mathcal T$ with noncyclic pointwise stabilizer has constant traversal sign. The positive successors of $e$ that can occur in such a path have the form $pte$, with $pI\in P/I$, and the positive predecessors have the form $pt^{-1}e$, with $pJ\in P/J$. Their common stabilizers with $e$ are, respectively, $$pIp^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad pJp^{-1}.$$ For a positive successor the coordinate transfer is $c\mapsto t^{-1}p^{-1}cpt$; for a positive predecessor it is $c\mapsto tp^{-1}cpt^{-1}$, on the indicated domains.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ann-frames"><summary>Proof of stmt-ann-frames</summary>

*Proof.* Every positively oriented edge leaving $H$ is $ate$ for some $a\in H$; the possible edges are parametrized by $aB\in H/B$. Its common stabilizer with $e$ is $A\cap aBa^{-1}$. If this intersection is noncyclic, assumption <span class="upright">(i)</span> gives $a\in AB$. Write $a=pb$ with $p\in P$ and $b\in B$. Since $bt=t\phi^{-1}(b)$, the edge $ate$ is $pte$. Moreover, $$pte=qte
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 t^{-1}q^{-1}pt\in A
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q^{-1}p\in A\cap B=I.$$ The common stabilizer is $P\cap pBp^{-1}=pIp^{-1}$, and conjugating by $(pt)^{-1}$ gives the stated transfer.

A positive predecessor ending at $t^{-1}H$ is $t^{-1}ae$, with $aA\in H/A$. After conjugating its pair stabilizer by $t$, we obtain $B\cap aAa^{-1}$. Noncyclicity gives $a\in BA$; writing $a=br$ and $p=t^{-1}bt\in P$ puts the predecessor in the form $pt^{-1}e$. Two such edges agree exactly when $$q^{-1}p\in P\cap t^{-1}Pt
 =t^{-1}(B\cap A)t=J.$$ Their common stabilizer with $e$ is $pJp^{-1}$, and conjugation by $(pt^{-1})^{-1}$ is the claimed transfer.

At a reversal of traversal sign, the consecutive edge stabilizers, in the common vertex coordinates, are $M$ and $aMa^{-1}$ for the same $M\in\{A,B\}$. The edges are distinct because the path is reduced, so $a\notin M$. Assumption <span class="upright">(i)</span> makes their intersection at most cyclic. A noncyclic stabilizer of the whole path cannot lie in that intersection. Thus there are no reversals. ◻

</details>

We call a consecutive positive pair *aligned* when its bridge lies in the double coset $AB$ in the common vertex coordinates. Reversing the traversal gives the corresponding definition for a consecutive negative pair. Lemma <a href="#ann:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:frames">11</a> says that every path with noncyclic stabilizer is aligned after its traversal sign is fixed.

<a id="stmt-ann-window-bound"></a>
<div id="ann:window-bound" class="lemma">

<a id="ann:window-sum"></a>
**Lemma 12** (Rank bound for a fixed window). *Fix integers $\ell,r\geq0$. Consider the positively traversed reduced paths with edges indexed by $-\ell,\ldots,r$, with edge $0$ equal to $e$, and with noncyclic pointwise stabilizer. Then $$\sum_{[W]\in P\backslash\{\text{such paths}\}}
       \bar r\bigl(\operatorname{Stab}_G(W)\bigr)
 \leq\bar r(P).
 \label{ann:window-sum}$$ Every stabilizer in this sum is finitely generated and free. In particular, there are at most $\bar r(P)$ such $P$-orbits. Equivalently, the same bound holds for $G$-orbits if the distinguished edge is allowed to vary.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ann-window-bound"><summary>Proof of stmt-ann-window-bound</summary>

*Proof.* Build the fixed window by starting with $e$ and adding one edge at an endpoint at a time. The initial stabilizer is $P$. Suppose $W$ is an existing representative, $S=\operatorname{Stab}_G(W)\leq P$, and $h$ is a frame at the endpoint edge being extended. In that frame put $K=h^{-1}Sh\leq P$.

For a forward extension whose stabilizer is noncyclic, Lemma <a href="#ann:frames" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ann:frames">11</a> restricts the next edge to $hpte$, with $pI\in P/I$. The action of $S$ on these choices becomes left multiplication by $K$. Thus the extension orbits over the orbit of $W$ are indexed by the double cosets $KpI$, and the extended stabilizer is isomorphic to $K\cap pIp^{-1}$. Assumption <span class="upright">(ii)</span> gives $$\sum_{KpI}\bar r(K\cap pIp^{-1})\leq\bar r(K)=\bar r(S).$$ For backward extension use $J$ and its separately assumed inequality. The stabilizers remain finitely generated: in the current edge coordinates an extension takes the intersection of two finitely generated subgroups of the finite-rank free group $P$.

The forgetful map from extended windows to the preceding windows is $P$-equivariant. Its fiber over the orbit of $W$ is precisely the set of extensions modulo $S$, which is the double-coset set just considered. Summing the displayed inequality over the existing representatives therefore proves the induction step. Windows with cyclic stabilizer may be discarded, since extension only decreases a stabilizer. This proves <a href="#ann:window-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ann:window-sum">[ann:window-sum]</a>. Each noncyclic term contributes at least one, proving the orbit bound. Finally, $\operatorname{Stab}_G(e)=P$, which identifies the two stated orbit sets. ◻

</details>

The shape $(\ell,r)$ is fixed in this lemma; no bound is asserted for the union of windows of all lengths. We shall also use the following direct consequence of the same hypotheses.

<a id="stmt-ann-distinct-paths"></a>
<div id="ann:distinct-paths" class="lemma">

**Lemma 13**. *Two distinct aligned paths of the same fixed window shape with distinguished edge $e$ and the same traversal sign have at most cyclic common pointwise stabilizer.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ann-distinct-paths"><summary>Proof of stmt-ann-distinct-paths</summary>

*Proof.* At their first divergence from the common part, the common stabilizer, in the shared endpoint frame, lies in $$pEp^{-1}\cap qEq^{-1},\qquad pE\ne qE,
 \qquad E\in\{I,J\}.$$ This intersection is at most cyclic. Indeed, apply <a href="#ann:inertia-assumption" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ann:inertia-assumption">[ann:inertia-assumption]</a> with $S=E$. The diagonal double coset already contributes $\bar r(E)$, so every other term has reduced rank zero. Since $p^{-1}q\notin E$, the displayed intersection is one of these off-diagonal intersections up to conjugacy. If $E$ is cyclic or trivial, the conclusion is immediate as well. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### The algebraic annular-flaring criterion

We state the external combination theorem with its quantifiers before passing to limits. This is important because the width bound in a sequence of counterexamples will depend on the number of rows.

<a id="ann:row-equation"></a>
For a traversal sign $\epsilon\in\{+,-\}$ define the arriving and departing maps into $H$ by $$f_+(c)=c,\qquad f_-(c)=tct^{-1},\qquad
 l_+(c)=tct^{-1},\qquad l_-(c)=c.$$ An *algebraic annulus with $2m+1$ rows* consists of signs $\epsilon_s\in\{+,-\}$ and elements $c_s\in P\setminus\{1\}$ for $-m\leq s\leq m$, together with bridges $a_s\in H$ for $-m\leq s<m$, satisfying $$f_{\epsilon_s}(c_s)
   =a_s l_{\epsilon_{s+1}}(c_{s+1})a_s^{-1}.
 \label{ann:row-equation}$$ Its width is $\max_s|a_s|_H$ and its girth is $|c_0|_P$. It is *essential* if a $+,-$ transition has $a_s\notin A$ and a $-,+$ transition has $a_s\notin B$. There is no restriction at equal-sign transitions. It is $\lambda$-*hyperbolic* if $$\max\{|c_{-m}|_P,|c_m|_P\}\geq\lambda|c_0|_P.$$ All these lengths are based word lengths.

<a id="ann:frame-equation"></a>
For completeness, these definitions describe an actual reduced tree path. Write $f_{\epsilon_s}(c)=u^{-1}cu$ and $l_{\epsilon_{s+1}}(c)=v^{-1}cv$, where $u,v\in\{1,t^{-1}\}$. Starting with a frame $h_s$, choose $$h_{s+1}=h_su a_sv^{-1}.
 \label{ann:frame-equation}$$ The two edges meet at $h_suH=h_{s+1}vH$, and <a href="#ann:row-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ann:row-equation">[ann:row-equation]</a> gives $h_sc_sh_s^{-1}=h_{s+1}c_{s+1}h_{s+1}^{-1}$. Thus a single nontrivial element fixes the developed path. The four possible frame multipliers are $$\begin{array}{c|cccc}
 (\epsilon_s,\epsilon_{s+1})&(+,+)&(+,-)&(-,+)&(-,-)\\ \hline
 u a_sv^{-1}&a_st&a_s&t^{-1}a_st&t^{-1}a_s.
\end{array}$$ At the two reversing transitions the consecutive geometric edges agree exactly when $a_s\in A$ or $a_s\in B$, respectively. Essentiality is therefore exactly absence of backtracking.

<a id="stmt-ann-external-flaring"></a>
<div id="ann:external-flaring" class="theorem">

**Theorem 14** (Algebraic annular flaring). *Let $H$ be word-hyperbolic, let $A,B\leq H$ be finitely generated, and let $\phi:A\to B$ be an isomorphism such that both attaching maps $A\to H$ are quasi-isometric embeddings. Set $G=\langle H,t\mid tat^{-1}=\phi(a),\ a\in A\rangle$. Then $G$ is word-hyperbolic if there exist $\lambda>1$ and an integer $m\geq1$ such that, for every $\rho>0$, there is $h(\rho)$ for which every essential algebraic annulus as defined above, with $2m+1$ rows, width at most $\rho$, and girth at least $h(\rho)$, is $\lambda$-hyperbolic.*

</div>

This is the algebraic formulation of the Bestvina–Feighn combination theorem in (Bestvina and Feighn 1996, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BestvinaFeighn1996Addendum](#ref-BestvinaFeighn1996Addendum)]; we use its explicit annular statement in (Kapovich 1999, Definitions 4.1–4.4 and Theorem 4.5) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Kapovich1999NonQuasiconvexity](#ref-Kapovich1999NonQuasiconvexity)]. The latter numbers refer to the arXiv version of that paper. Our $2m+1$ counts the successive edge groups, including both ends. The criterion applies here because $A$ and $B$ are quasiconvex in the hyperbolic group $H$.

<a id="stmt-ann-counterannuli"></a>
<div id="ann:counterannuli" class="lemma">

**Lemma 15** (Counterannuli at a fixed length). *If $G$ is not hyperbolic, then for every $m\geq1$ there is a sequence of essential annuli with $2m+1$ rows whose signs and bridges are fixed throughout the sequence, such that $$|c_0|_P\longrightarrow\infty,
